| BY WILLIAM SHAKESPEARE AND | GEORGE WILKINS A RECONSTRUCTED TEXT |
| ON | 20 May 1608 [[Pericles]] was entered on the Stationers’ Register to Edward Blount; but he did not publish it. Probably the players allowed him to license it in the [[Hope\|hope]] of preventing its publication by anyone else, for it was one of the most popular plays of the period. Its [[success]] was exploited, also in 1608, by the publication of a [[Novel\|novel]], by George Wilkins, ‘The Painful Adventures of [[Pericles]] Prince of Tyre, Being the True [[history\|History]] of the Play of [[Pericles]], as it was lately presented by the worthy and ancient poet John [[Gower]]’. The play itself appeared in print in the following year, with an ascription to Shakespeare, but in a manifestly corrupt text that gives every sign of having been put together from [[Memory\|memory]]. This quarto was several times reprinted, but the play was not included in the 1623 Folio (perhaps because Heminges and Condell knew that Shakespeare was responsible for only part of it).<br>In putting together The Painful Adventures, Wilkins drew on an earlier version of the tale, The Pattern of Painful Adventures, by Laurence Twine, written in the mid-1570s and reprinted in 1607. Twine’s book is also a source of the play, which draws too on the story of [[Apollonius]] of Tyre as told by John [[Gower]] in his Confessio Amantis, and, to a lesser extent, on Sir Philip Sidney’s Arcadia. Wilkins not only incorporated verbatim passages from Twine’s book, he also drew heavily on [[Pericles]] itself. Since the play text is so corrupt, it is quite likely that Wilkins reports parts of it both more accurately and more fully than the quarto. And he may have had special qualifications for doing so. He was a dramatist whose popular play The Miseries of Enforced [[Marriage]] had been performed by Shakespeare’s company. [[Pericles]] has usually been regarded as either a collaborative play or one in which Shakespeare revised a pre-existing script. Our edition is based on the hypothesis (not new) that Wilkins was its joint author. Our attempt to reconstruct the play draws more heavily than is usual on Wilkins’s [[Novel\|novel]], especially in the first nine scenes (which he probably wrote); in general, because of its obvious corruption, the original text is more freely emended than usual. So that readers may experience the play as originally printed, an unemended reprint of the 1609 quarto is given in our original-spelling edition. The deficiencies of the text are in part compensated for by the [[Survival\|survival]] of an unusual amount of relevant visual material, reproduced overleaf.<br>The complex textual background of [[Pericles]] should not be allowed to draw attention away from the merits of this dramatic [[Romance\|romance]], which we [[Hope\|hope]] will be more apparent as the result of our treatment of the text. If the original play had survived, it might [[Well\|well]] have been as highly valued as The [[Winter]]’s Tale or The Tempest; as it is, it contains some hauntingly beautiful episodes, above all that in Scene 21 in which [[Marina]], [[Pericles]]’ long-lost daughter, draws him out of the comatose state to which his sufferings have reduced him.<br><br>14. From the title-page of The Painful Adventures of [[Pericles]] Prince of Tyre (1608), by George Wilkins; [[Artist\|artist]] unknown. Since [[Gower]] is not a character in Wilkins’s [[Novel\|novel]], the choice of woodcut undoubtedly reflects both the play’s popularity and [[Gower]]’s own impact in early performances, and it is as likely to reflect the visual detail of performance as any early title-page. The sprig of laurel (or posy) in [[Gower]]’s left hand is symbolic of his poetic status.<br><br>15. From Greene’s Vision (1592), sig. CIr―CIv; probably by Robert Greene. The description here fits reasonably [[Well\|well]] the Painful Adventures title-page, though the woodcut does not contain the ‘bag of red’, ‘napkin’, or tight-fitting ‘breech’.<br><br>16. Severed heads displayed on the gate of [[London]] Bridge, from an etching by Claes Jan Visscher (1616). In the play’s sources, and Painful Adventures, the heads of previous suitors (Sc. 1) are placed on the ’gate’ of Antioch. In performance they could have been thrust out on poles from the upper stage; but the timing and method of their display is not clear.<br><br>17.<br>From The Heroical Devices of M. [[Claudius]] Paradin, translated by P.S. (1591), sig. V3. This is the source for the impresa of the Third Knight, in Sc. 6.<br><br>18. From The Heroical Devices of M. [[Claudius]] Paradin, translated by P.S. (1591), sig. Z3. This is the source for the impresa of the Fourth Knight, in Sc. 6.<br><br>19. An Inigo [[Jones]] [[Sketch\|sketch]] of [[Diana]], probably for Ben Jonson’s [[Masque\|masque]] [[Time]] Vindicated (1623). The goddess of chastity appeared as a character in [[Court\|court]] entertainments, masques, and plays, and her representation was governed by iconographic convention. As goddess of hunting, she was most often identified by her ‘silver bow’ (21.234). In Thomas Heywood’s The [[Golden Age]] (1611), stage directions refer to ‘[[Diana]]’s bow’ (sig. EIv) and her ‘buskins’ (sig. E3v); her ‘nymphs’ explicitly, and by inference she, have ‘garlands on their heads, and javelins in their hands ... bows and quivers’ (sig. D3v). The bow, quiver, and javelin, all visible in [[Jones]]’s [[Sketch\|sketch]], were commonplace in emblematic representations. As a huntress, [[Diana]] could naturally be envisaged in a chariot: in Aurelian Townshend’s [[Masque\|masque]] Albion’s Triumph (1631), she descends ‘in her chariot’ (pp. 2, 12); in [[Time]] Vindicated, ‘[[Diana]] descends’ (1. 446). Such descents for deities were used in the public theatres, too, usually in a chair or chariot (21.224.2).<br><br>20. A miniature of [[Diana]] by Isaac [[Oliver]] (1615): the dress is yellow, the scarf a gauzy pink-white, the cloak over her right shoulder blue; the leaf-shaped brooch topped by the crescent [[Moon\|moon]], gold. In Samuel Daniel’s [[Masque\|masque]] The Vision of the Twelve Goddesses (1604), ‘[[Diana]], in a green mantle embroidered with silver half moons, and a crescent of pearl on her head, presents a bow and quiver’ (sig. A5). The ‘crescent of pearl’—an ornamental crescent [[Moon\|moon]], also detectable in [[Jones]]’s [[Sketch\|sketch]]—can be seen in many emblematic representations of the goddess.<br><br>21. For the [[Pastoral\|pastoral]] Florimène (1635), Inigo [[Jones]] designed two scenic views of ’The Temple of [[Diana]]’ (see 1. 22.17.1). Though such scenes were not used in the public theatres in Shakespeare’s [[Time\|time]], the columns supporting the overhanging roof of the public stage (see General Introduction, pp. xxvii-xxix) could have created a scenic effect roughly similar to [[Jones]]’s recessed classical temple. Statues were also available as props in the public theatre; in [[Pericles]], as in The [[Winter]]’s Tale, the statue could have been impersonated by an actor on a pedestal. Whether or not a statue was visible, the temple could be identified by an altar (as in The Two Noble Kinsmen). |
| THE PERSONS OF THE | PLAY<br>John [[Gower\|GOWER]], the Presenter<br><br>[[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]], [[King]] of Antioch<br>His DAUGHTER<br>THALIART, a [[Villain\|villain]]<br><br>[[Pericles\|PERICLES]], Prince of Tyre<br>[[Marina\|MARINA]], [[Pericles]]’ daughter<br><br>[[Cleon\|CLEON]], Governor of Tarsus<br>DIONIZA, his wife<br>LEONINE, a murderer |
| [[King\|KING]] | [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]], of Pentapolis<br>[[Thaisa\|THAISA]], his daughter<br>Three FISHERMEN, his subjects<br>Five PRINCES, suitors of [[Thaisa]]<br>A MARSHAL<br>LYCHORIDA, [[Thaisa]]’s [[Nurse\|nurse]]<br><br>CERIMON, a physician of Ephesus<br>PHILEMON, his servant<br><br>LYSIMACHUS, Governor of Mytilene<br>A BAWD<br>A PANDER<br>BOULT, a leno<br><br>[[Diana\|DIANA]], goddess of chastity<br><br>Lords, ladies, pages, messengers, sailors, gentlemen<br><br>A Reconstructed Text of [[Pericles]], Prince of Tyre<br><br>Sc. 1 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Gower]] as Prologue |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | To sing a [[Song\|song]] that old was sung<br>From ashes ancient [[Gower]] is come,<br>Assuming man’s infirmities<br>To glad your ear and please your eyes.<br>It hath been sung at festivals,<br>On ember-eves and holy-ales,<br>And lords and ladies in their lives<br>Have read it for restoratives.<br>The purchase is to make men glorious,<br>Et bonum quo antiquius eo melius.<br><br>If you, born in these latter times<br>When wit’s more ripe, accept my rhymes,<br>And that to hear an old man sing<br>May to your wishes pleasure bring,<br>I life would wish, and that I might<br>Waste it for you like taper-light.<br>This’ Antioch, then; [[Antiochus]] the Great<br>Built up this [[City\|city]] for his chiefest seat,<br>The fairest in all Syria.<br>I tell you what mine authors say.<br>This [[King\|king]] unto him took a fere<br>Who died, and left a female heir<br>So buxom, blithe, and full of face<br>As heav’n had lent her all his grace,<br>With whom the father liking took,<br>And her to incest did provoke.<br>Bad [[Child\|child]], worse father, to entice his own<br>To evil should be done by none.<br>By custom what they did begin<br>Was with long use account’ no sin.<br>The [[Beauty\|beauty]] of this sinful dame<br>Made many princes thither frame<br>To seek her as a bedfellow,<br>In [[Marriage\|marriage]] pleasures playfellow,<br>Which to prevent he made a law<br>To keep her still, and men in awe,<br>That whoso asked her for his wife,<br>His [[Riddle\|riddle]] told not, lost his life.<br>So for her many a wight did die, |
| *(didascalia)* | A row of heads is revealed |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | As yon grim looks do testify.<br>What now ensues, to th’ judgement of your eye<br>I give, my cause who best can justify. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Sennet. Enter [[King]] [[Antiochus]], Prince [[Pericles]], and lords and peers in their richest ornaments |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Young Prince of Tyre, you have at large received<br>The danger of the task you undertake. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I have, [[Antiochus]], and with a [[Soul\|soul]]<br>Emboldened with the glory of her praise<br>Think [[Death\|death]] no hazard in this enterprise. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Music! |
| *(didascalia)* | Music sounds |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Bring in our daughter, clothèd like a bride<br>Fit for th’embracements ev’n of Jove himself,<br>At whose conception, till Lucina reigned,<br>[[Nature]] this dowry gave to glad her presence:<br>The senate-[[house]] of planets all did sit,<br>In her their best perfections to knit. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Antiochus]]’ Daughter |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | See where she comes, apparelled like the [[spring]],<br>Graces her subjects, and her thoughts the [[King\|king]]<br>Of ev’ry virtue gives renown to men;<br>Her face the book of praises, where is read<br>Nothing but curious pleasures, as from thence<br>Sorrow were ever razed and testy wrath<br>Could never be her mild companion.<br>You gods that made me man, and sway in [[Love\|love]],<br>That have inflamed [[Desire\|desire]] in my breast<br>To taste the fruit of yon celestial tree<br>Or die in the adventure, be my helps,<br>As I am son and servant to your will,<br>To compass such a boundless happiness. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Prince [[Pericles]]— |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | That would be son to great [[Antiochus]]. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Before thee stands this fair Hesperides,<br>With golden fruit, but dang’rous to be touched, |
| *(didascalia)* | He gestures towards the heads |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | For [[Death\|death]]-like dragons here affright thee hard. |
| *(didascalia)* | He gestures towards his daughter |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Her heav‘n-like face enticeth thee to view<br>Her countless glory, which [[Desert\|desert]] must gain;<br>And which without [[Desert\|desert]], because thine eye<br>Presumes to reach, all the whole heap must die.<br>Yon sometimes famous princes, like thyself<br>Drawn by report, advent’rous by [[Desire\|desire]],<br>Tell thee with speechless tongues and semblants<br>bloodless<br>That without covering save yon field of [[Stars\|stars]]<br>Here they stand, martyrs slain in Cupid’s wars,<br>And with dead cheeks advise thee to desist<br>From going on [[Death\|death]]’s net, whom none resist. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | [[Antiochus]], I thank thee, who hath taught<br>My frail [[Mortality\|mortality]] to know itself,<br>And by those fearful objects to prepare<br>This [[Body\|body]], like to them, to what I must;<br>For [[Death\|death]] remembered should be like a [[Mirror\|mirror]]<br>Who tells us life’s but breath, to trust it error.<br>I’ll make my will then, and, as sick men do,<br>Who know the world, see heav‘n, but feeling woe<br>Grip not at earthly joys as erst they did,<br>So I bequeath a happy peace to you<br>And all good men, as ev’ry prince should do;<br>My riches to the earth from whence they came,<br>(To the Daughter) But my unspotted [[Fire\|fire]] of [[Love\|love]] to you.<br>(To [[Antiochus]]) Thus ready for the way of life or [[Death\|death]],<br>I wait the sharpest blow, [[Antiochus]]. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Scorning advice, read the conclusion then, |
| *(didascalia)* | He angrily throws down the [[Riddle\|riddle]] |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Which read and not expounded, ’tis decreed,<br>As these before thee, thou thyself shalt bleed. |
| DAUGHTER | (to [[Pericles]])<br>Of all ‘sayed yet, mayst thou prove prosperous;<br>Of all ’sayed yet, I wish thee happiness. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Like a bold champion I assume the lists,<br>Nor ask advice of any other thought<br>But faithfulness and [[Courage\|courage]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | He takes up and reads aloud the [[Riddle\|riddle]] |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I am no viper, yet I feed<br>On mother’s flesh which did me breed.<br>I sought a husband, in which labour<br>I found that kindness in a father.<br>He’s father, son, and husband mild;<br>I mother, wife, and yet his [[Child\|child]].<br>How this may be and yet in two,<br>As you will live resolve it you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sharp physic is the last. Aside But O, you powers |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | That gives heav’n countless eyes to view men’s acts,<br>Why cloud they not their sights perpetually<br>If this be true which makes me pale to read it? |
| *(didascalia)* | He gazes on the Daughter |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Fair glass of light, I loved you, and could still,<br>Were not this glorious casket stored with ill.<br>But I must tell you now my thoughts revolt,<br>For he’s no man on whom perfections wait<br>That, knowing sin within, will touch the gate.<br>You’re a fair viol, and your sense the strings<br>Who, fingered to make man his lawful music,<br>Would draw heav’n down and all the gods to hearken,<br>But, being played upon before your [[Time\|time]],<br>Hell only danceth at so harsh a chime.<br>Good sooth, I care not for you. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Prince [[Pericles]], touch not, upon thy life,<br>For that’s an article within our law<br>As dang’rous as the rest. Your [[Time\|time]]’s expired.<br>Either expound now, or receive your sentence. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Great [[King]],<br>Few [[Love\|love]] to hear the sins they [[Love\|love]] to act.<br>‘Twould braid yourself too near for me to tell it.<br>Who has a book of all that monarchs do,<br>He’s more secure to keep it shut than shown,<br>For vice repeated, like the wand’ring [[Wind\|wind]],<br>Blows [[Dust\|dust]] in others’ eyes to spread itself;<br>And yet the end of all is bought thus dear,<br>The breath is gone, and the sore eyes see clear<br>To stop the air would hurt them. The blind mole casts<br>Copped hills towards heav’n to tell the earth is thronged<br>By man’s oppression, and the poor worm doth die for’t.<br>Kings are earth’s gods; in vice their law’s their will,<br>And if Jove stray, who dares say Jove doth ill?<br>It is enough you know, and it is fit,<br>What being more known grows worse, to smother it.<br>All [[Love\|love]] the womb that their first being bred;<br>Then give my tongue like leave to [[Love\|love]] my head. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | (aside)<br>Heav’n, that I had thy head! He’s found the meaning.<br>But I will gloze with him.—Young Prince of Tyre,<br>Though by the tenor of our strict edict,<br>Your exposition misinterpreting,<br>We might proceed to cancel of your days,<br>Yet [[Hope\|hope]], succeeding from so fair a tree<br>As your fair [[Self\|self]], doth tune us otherwise.<br>Forty days longer we do respite you,<br>If by which [[Time\|time]] our secret be undone,<br>This [[Mercy\|mercy]] shows we’ll joy in such a son.<br>And until then your entertain shall be<br>As doth befit your worth and our degree. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish.Exeunt all but [[Pericles]] |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | How courtesy would seem to cover sin<br>When what is done is like an hypocrite,<br>The which is good in nothing but in sight.<br>If it be true that I interpret false,<br>Then were it certain you were not so bad<br>As with foul incest to abuse your [[Soul\|soul]],<br>Where now you’re both a father and a son<br>By your uncomely claspings with your [[Child\|child]]—<br>Which pleasures fits a husband, not a father—<br>And she, an eater of her mother’s flesh,<br>By the defiling of her parents’ bed,<br>And both like serpents are, who though they feed<br>On sweetest [[Flowers\|flowers]], yet they [[Poison\|poison]] breed.<br>Antioch, farewell, for wisdom sees those men<br>Blush not in actions blacker than the [[Night\|night]]<br>Will ’schew no course to keep them from the light.<br>One sin, I know, another doth provoke.<br>Murder’s as near to lust as flame to smoke.<br>[[Poison]] and treason are the hands of sin,<br>Ay, and the targets to put off the shame.<br>Then, lest my life be cropped to keep you clear,<br>By flight I’ll shun the danger which I [[Fear\|fear]]. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Antiochus]] |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | He hath found the meaning, for the which we mean<br>To have his head. He must not live<br>To trumpet forth my infamy, nor tell the world<br>[[Antiochus]] doth sin in such a loathèd manner,<br>And therefore instantly this prince must die,<br>For by his fall my [[Honour\|honour]] must keep high.<br>Who attends us there? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Thaliart |
| THALIART | Doth your highness call? |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Thaliart, you are of our chamber, Thaliart,<br>And to your secrecy our mind partakes<br>Her private actions. For your faithfulness<br>We will advance you, Thaliart. Behold,<br>Here’s [[Poison\|poison]], and here’s gold.<br>We hate the Prince of Tyre, and thou must kill him.<br>It fits thee not to ask the reason. Why?<br>Because we bid it. Say, is it done? |
| THALIART | My lord, ’tis done. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Enough. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger hastily |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Let your breath cool yourself, telling your haste. |
| *(didascalia)* | Your majesty, Prince [[Pericles]] is fled. Exit |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | (to Thaliart)<br>As thou wilt live, fly after; like an arrow<br>Shot from a [[Well\|well]]-experienced archer hits<br>The mark his eye doth level at, so thou<br>Never return unless it be to say<br>‘Your majesty, Prince [[Pericles]] is dead.’ |
| THALIART | If I can get him in my [[Pistol\|pistol]]’s length<br>I’ll make him sure enough. Farewell, your highness. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Thaliart, adieu. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Thaliart |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Till [[Pericles]] be dead<br>My heart can lend no succour to my head. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit. The heads are concealed |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Sc. 2 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Pericles]], distempered, with his lords |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Let none disturb us. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt lords |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Why should this change of thoughts,<br>The sad companion, dull-eyed [[Melancholy\|melancholy]],<br>Be my so used a guest as not an hour<br>In the day’s glorious walk or peaceful [[Night\|night]],<br>The tomb where grief should [[Sleep\|sleep]], can breed me<br>quiet? 5<br>Here pleasures [[Court\|court]] mine eyes, and mine eyes shun<br>them,<br>And danger, which I feared, ’s at Antioch,<br>Whose arm seems far too short to hit me here.<br>Yet neither pleasure’s art can joy my spirits,<br>Nor yet care’s author’s distance comfort me.<br>Then it is thus: the passions of the mind,<br>That have their first conception by misdread,<br>Have after-nourishment and life by care,<br>And what was first but [[Fear\|fear]] what might be done<br>Grows elder now, and cares it be not done.<br>And so with me. The great [[Antiochus]],<br>‘Gainst whom I am too little to contend,<br>Since he’s so great can make his will his act,<br>Will think me speaking though I swear to [[Silence\|silence]],<br>Nor boots it me to say I [[Honour\|honour]] him<br>If he suspect I may dishonour him.<br>And what may make him blush in being known,<br>He’ll stop the course by which it might be known.<br>With hostile forces he’ll o’erspread the land,<br>And with th‘ostent of war will look so huge<br>Amazement shall drive [[Courage\|courage]] from the state,<br>Our men be vanquished ere they do resist,<br>And subjects punished that ne’er thought offence,<br>Which care of them, not pity of myself,<br>Who am no more but as the tops of trees<br>Which fence the roots they grow by and defend them,<br>Makes both my [[Body\|body]] pine and [[Soul\|soul]] to languish,<br>And punish that before that he would punish. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter all the Lords, among them old Helicanus, to [[Pericles]] |
| FIRST LORD | Joy and all comfort in your sacred breast! |
| SECOND LORD | And keep your mind peaceful and comfortable. |
| HELICANUS | Peace, peace, and give experience tongue.<br>(To [[Pericles]]) You do not [[Well\|well]] so to abuse yourself,<br>To waste your [[Body\|body]] here with pining sorrow,<br>Upon whose safety doth depend the lives<br>And the prosperity of a whole kingdom.<br>‘Tis ill in you to do it, and no less<br>ll in your council not to contradict it.<br>They do abuse the [[King]] that flatter him,<br>For flatt’ry is the bellows blows up sin;<br>The thing the which is flattered, but a spark,<br>To which that [[Wind\|wind]] gives heat and stronger glowing;<br>Whereas reproof, obedient and in order,<br>Fits kings as they are men, for they may err.<br>When Signor Sooth here does proclaim a peace<br>He flatters you, makes war upon your life. |
| *(didascalia)* | He kneels |
| HELICANUS | Prince, pardon me, or strike me if you please.<br>I cannot be much lower than my knees. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | All leave us else; but let your cares o’erlook<br>What shipping and what lading’s in our haven,<br>And then return to us. Exeunt Lords<br>Helicane, thou<br>Hast moved us. What seest thou in our looks? |
| HELICANUS | An angry brow, dread lord. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | If there be such a dart in princes’ frowns,<br>How durst thy tongue move anger to our brows? |
| HELICANUS | How dares the plants look up to heav’n from whence<br>They have their nourishment? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Thou knowest I have pow’r to take thy life from thee. |
| HELICANUS | I have ground the axe myself; do you but strike the blow. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] lifting him up |
| HELICANUS | Rise, prithee, rise. Sit down. Thou art no flatterer,<br>I thank thee for it, and the heav‘ns forbid<br>That kings should let their ears hear their faults hid.<br>Fit counsellor and servant for a prince,<br>Who by thy wisdom mak’st a prince thy servant,<br>What wouldst thou have me do? |
| HELICANUS | To bear with patience<br>Such griefs as you do lay upon yourself. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Thou speak‘st like a physician, Helicanus,<br>That ministers a potion unto me<br>That thou wouldst tremble to receive thyself.<br>Attend me, then. I went to Antioch,<br>Where, as thou know’st, against the face of [[Death\|death]]<br>I sought the purchase of a glorious [[Beauty\|beauty]]<br>From whence an issue I might propagate,<br>As children are heav‘n’s blessings: to parents,<br>objects;<br>Are arms to princes, and bring joys to subjects.<br>Her face was to mine eye beyond all [[Wonder\|wonder]],<br>The rest—hark in thine ear—as black as incest,<br>Which by my knowledge found, the sinful father<br>Seemed not to strike, but smooth. But thou know’st<br>this,<br>‘Tis [[Time\|time]] to [[Fear\|fear]] when tyrants seems to kiss;<br>Which [[Fear\|fear]] so grew in me I hither fled<br>Under the covering of careful [[Night\|night]],<br>Who seemed my good protector, and being here<br>Bethought me what was past, what might succeed.<br>I knew him tyrannous, and tyrants’ fears<br>Decrease not, but grow faster than the years.<br>And should he [[Doubt\|doubt]]—as [[Doubt\|doubt]] no [[Doubt\|doubt]] he doth—<br>That I should open to the list’ning air<br>How many worthy princes’ bloods were shed<br>To keep his bed of blackness unlaid ope,<br>To lop that [[Doubt\|doubt]] he’ll fill this land with arms,<br>And make pretence of wrong that I have done him,<br>When all for mine—if I may call—offence<br>Must feel war’s blow, who spares not [[innocence]];<br>Which [[Love\|love]] to all, of which thyself art one,<br>Who now reproved’st me for’t— |
| HELICANUS | Alas, sir. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Drew [[Sleep\|sleep]] out of mine eyes, [[Blood\|blood]] from my cheeks,<br>Musings into my mind, with thousand doubts,<br>How I might stop this tempest ere it came,<br>And, finding little comfort to relieve them,<br>I thought it princely [[Charity\|charity]] to grieve them. |
| HELICANUS | [[Well]], my lord, since you have giv’n me leave to speak,<br>Freely will I speak. [[Antiochus]] you [[Fear\|fear]],<br>And justly too, I think, you [[Fear\|fear]] the [[Tyrant\|tyrant]],<br>Who either by public war or private treason<br>Will take away your life.<br>Therefore, my lord, go travel for a while,<br>Till that his rage and anger be forgot,<br>Or destinies do cut his thread of life.<br>Your rule direct to any; if to me,<br>Day serves not light more faithful than I’ll be. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I do not [[Doubt\|doubt]] thy [[Faith\|faith]],<br>But should he in my absence wrong thy liberties? |
| HELICANUS | We’ll mingle our bloods together in the earth<br>From whence we had our being and our birth. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Tyre, I now look from thee then, and to Tarsus<br>Intend my travel, where I’ll hear from thee,<br>And by whose letters I’ll dispose myself.<br>The care I had and have of subjects’ good<br>On thee I lay, whose wisdom’s strength can bear it.<br>I’ll take thy word for [[Faith\|faith]], not ask thine oath;<br>Who shuns not to break one will sure crack both.<br>But in our orbs we’ll live so round and safe<br>That [[Time\|time]] of both this [[Truth\|truth]] shall ne’er convince:<br>Thou showed’st a subject’s shine, I a true prince. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Sc. 3 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Thaliart |
| THALIART | So this is Tyre, and this the [[Court\|court]]. Here must I kill [[King]] [[Pericles]], and if I do it and am caught I am like to be hanged abroad, but if I do it not, I am sure to be hanged at [[Home\|home]]. ‘Tis dangerous. [[Well]], I perceive he was a wise fellow and had good discretion that, being bid to ask what he would of the [[King]], desired he might know none of his [[Secrets\|secrets]]. Now do I see he had some reason for’t, for if a [[King\|king]] bid a man be a [[Villain\|villain]], he’s bound by the indenture of his oath to be one. Hush, here comes the lords of Tyre. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Helicanus and Aeschines, with other lords |
| HELICANUS | You shall not need, my fellow peers of Tyre,<br>Further to question of your [[King]]’s departure.<br>His sealed commission left in trust with me<br>Does speak sufficiently he’s gone to travel. |
| THALIART | (aside) How? The [[King]] gone? |
| HELICANUS | If further yet you will be satisfied<br>Why, as it were unlicensed of your loves,<br>He would depart, I’ll give some light unto you.<br>Being at Antioch— |
| THALIART | (aside)<br>What from Antioch? |
| HELICANUS | Royal [[Antiochus]], on what cause I know not,<br>Took some displeasure at him—at least he judged so—<br>And doubting lest that he had erred or sinned,<br>To show his sorrow he’d correct himself;<br>So puts himself unto the [[Ship\|ship]]-man’s toil,<br>With whom each minute threatens life or [[Death\|death]]. |
| THALIART | (aside)<br>[[Well]], I perceive I shall not be hanged now,<br>Although I would.<br>But since he’s gone, the [[King]]’s ears it must please<br>He scaped the land to perish on the seas.<br>I’ll present myself.—Peace to the lords of Tyre.<br>Lord Thaliart am I, of Antioch. |
| *(didascalia)* | HELICANUS |
| THALIART | Lord Thaliart of Antioch is welcome. |
| THALIART | From [[King]] [[Antiochus]] I come<br>With message unto princely [[Pericles]],<br>But since my landing I have understood<br>Your lord’s betook himself to unknown travels.<br>Now my message must return from whence it came. |
| HELICANUS | We have no reason to enquire it,<br>Commended to our master, not to us.<br>Yet ere you shall depart, this we [[Desire\|desire]]:<br>As friends to Antioch, we may feast in Tyre. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 4 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Cleon]], the Governor of Tarsus, with [[Dionyza]] his wife, and others |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | My [[Dionyza]], shall we rest us here<br>And, by relating tales of others’ griefs,<br>See if ’twill teach us to forget our own? |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | That were to blow at [[Fire\|fire]] in [[Hope\|hope]] to quench it,<br>For who digs hills because they do aspire<br>Throws down one mountain to cast up a higher.<br>O my distressed lord, e’en such our griefs are;<br>Here they’re but felt and seen with midges’ eyes,<br>But like to groves, being topped they higher rise. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | O [[Dionyza]],<br>Who wanteth food and will not say he wants it,<br>Or can conceal his hunger till he famish?<br>Our tongues our sorrows dictate to sound deep<br>Our woes into the air, our eyes to weep<br>Till lungs fetch breath that may proclaim them louder,<br>That, if heav’n slumber while their creatures want,<br>They may awake their helps to comfort them.<br>I’ll then discourse our woes, felt sev’ral years,<br>And, wanting breath to speak, help me with [[Tears\|tears]]. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | As you think best, sir. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | This Tarsus o‘er which I have the [[government]],<br>A [[City\|city]] o’er whom plenty held full hand,<br>For riches strewed herself ev’n in the streets,<br>Whose tow‘rs bore heads so high they kissed the clouds,<br>And strangers ne’er beheld but wondered at,<br>Whose men and dames so jetted and adorned<br>Like one another’s glass to trim them by;<br>Their tables were stored full to glad the sight,<br>And not so much to feed on as delight.<br>All [[Poverty\|poverty]] was scorned, and [[Pride\|pride]] so great<br>The name of help grew odious to repeat. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | O, ’tis too true. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | But see what heav’n can do by this our change.<br>Those mouths who but of late earth, sea, and air<br>Were all too little to content and please,<br>Although they gave their creatures in abundance,<br>As houses are defiled for want of use,<br>They are now starved for want of exercise.<br>Those palates who, not yet two summers younger,<br>Must have inventions to delight the taste<br>Would now be glad of bread and beg for it.<br>Those mothers who to nuzzle up their babes<br>Thought naught too curious are ready now<br>To eat those little darlings whom they loved.<br>So sharp are hunger’s teeth that man and wife<br>Draw lots who first shall die to lengthen life.<br>Here weeping stands a lord, there lies a lady dying,<br>Here many sink, yet those which see them fall<br>Have scarce strength left to give them burial.<br>Is not this true? |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | Our cheeks and hollow eyes do witness it. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | O, let those cities that of plenty’s cup<br>And her prosperities so largely taste<br>With their superfluous riots, heed these [[Tears\|tears]]!<br>The misery of Tarsus may be theirs. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a faintingLord of Tarsus slowly |
| LORD | Where’s the Lord Governor? |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | Here. Speak out thy sorrows which thou bring‘st in<br>haste,<br>For comfort is too far for us t’expect. |
| LORD | We have descried upon our neighbouring shore<br>A portly sail of ships make hitherward. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | I thought as much.<br>One sorrow never comes but brings an heir<br>That may succeed as his inheritor,<br>And so in ours. Some neighbour nation,<br>Taking advantage of our misery,<br>Hath stuffed these hollow vessels with their power<br>To beat us down, the which are down already,<br>And make a conquest of unhappy men,<br>Whereas no glory’s got to overcome. |
| LORD | That’s the least [[Fear\|fear]], for by the semblance<br>Of their white flags displayed they bring us peace,<br>And come to us as favourers, not foes. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | Thou speak‘st like him’s untutored to repeat;<br>Who makes the fairest show means most deceit.<br>But bring they what they will and what they can,<br>What need we [[Fear\|fear]]?<br>Our [[Grave\|grave]]’s the low’st, and we are half-way there.<br>Go tell their gen’ral we attend him here<br>To know for what he comes, and whence he comes. |
| LORD | I go, my lord. Exit |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | Welcome is peace, if he on peace consist;<br>If wars, we are unable to resist. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Lord again conducting [[Pericles]] with attendants |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | (to [[Cleon]])<br>Lord Governor, for so we hear you are,<br>Let not our ships and number of our men<br>Be like a beacon fixed t’amaze your eyes.<br>We have heard your miseries as far as Tyre,<br>Since entering your unshut gates have witnessed<br>The widowed desolation of your streets;<br>Nor come we to add sorrow to your hearts,<br>But to relieve them of their heavy load;<br>And these our ships, you happily may think<br>Are like the Trojan [[Horse\|horse]] was fraught within<br>With bloody veins importing overthrow,<br>Are stored with corn to make your needy bread,<br>And give them life whom hunger starved half dead. |
| *(didascalia)* | ALL OF TARSUS fallingon their knees and weeping |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | The gods of Greece protect you, and we’ll pray for you! |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Arise, I pray you, rise.<br>We do not look for reverence but for [[Love\|love]],<br>And harbourage for me, my ships and men. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | The which when any shall not gratify,<br>Or pay you with unthankfulness in thought,<br>Be it our wives, our children, or ourselves,<br>The curse of heav’n and men succeed their evils!<br>Till when—the which I [[Hope\|hope]] shall ne’er be seen—<br>Your grace is welcome to our town and us. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Which welcome we’ll accept, feast here a while,<br>Until our [[Stars\|stars]] that frown lend us a smile. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 5 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Gower]] |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | Here have you seen a mighty [[King\|king]]<br>His [[Child\|child]], iwis, to incest bring;<br>A better prince and benign lord<br>Prove awe-full both in deed and word.<br>Be quiet then, as men should be,<br>Till he hath passed necessity.<br>I’ll show you those in trouble’s reign,<br>Losing a mite, a mountain gain.<br>The good in conversation,<br>To whom I give my benison,<br>Is still at Tarsus where each man<br>Thinks all is writ he speken can,<br>And to remember what he does<br>His statue build to make him glorious.<br>But tidings to the contrary<br>Are brought your eyes. What need speak I?<br>Dumb show. Enter at one door [[Pericles]] talking with [[Cleon]], all the train with them. Enter at another door a gentleman with a letter to [[Pericles]]. [[Pericles]] shows the letter to [[Cleon]]. [[Pericles]] gives the messenger a reward, and knights him. Exeunt with their trains [[Pericles]] at one door and [[Cleon]] at another<br>Good Helicane that stayed at [[Home\|home]],<br>Not to eat honey like a drone<br>From others’ labours, for that he strive<br>To killen bad, keep good alive,<br>And to fulfil his prince’ [[Desire\|desire]]<br>Sent word of all that haps in Tyre;<br>How Thaliart came full bent with sin<br>And hid intent to murdren him,<br>And that in Tarsus was not best<br>Longer for him to make his rest.<br>He deeming so put forth to seas,<br>Where when men been there’s seldom ease,<br>For now the [[Wind\|wind]] begins to blow; |
| *(didascalia)* | Thunder above and deeps below |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | Makes such unquiet that the [[Ship\|ship]]<br>Should [[house]] him safe is wrecked and split,<br>And he, good prince, having all lost,<br>By waves from coast to coast is tossed.<br>All perishen of man, of pelf,<br>Ne aught escapend but himself,<br>Till fortune, tired with doing bad,<br>Threw him ashore to give him glad.<br>[Enter [[Pericles]] wet and half-naked]<br>And here he comes. What shall be next<br>Pardon old [[Gower]]; this ’longs the text. Exit<br>[Thunder and [[Lightning\|lightning]]] |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Yet cease your ire, you angry [[Stars\|stars]] of heaven! |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Wind]], rain, and thunder, remember earthly man |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Is but a substance that must yield to you,<br>And I, as fits my [[Nature\|nature]], do obey you.<br>Alas, the seas hath cast me on the rocks,<br>Washed me from shore to shore, and left my breath<br>Nothing to think on but ensuing [[Death\|death]].<br>Let it suffice the [[Greatness\|greatness]] of your powers<br>To have bereft a prince of all his fortunes,<br>And, having thrown him from your wat’ry [[Grave\|grave]],<br>Here to have [[Death\|death]] in peace is all he’ll crave.<br>He sits.Enter two poor Fishermen: one the Master, the other his man |
| MASTER | calling What ho, Pitch! |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | calling Ha, come and bring away the nets. |
| MASTER | calling What, Patchbreech, I say! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Third rough Fisherman with a hood upon his head and a filthy leathern pelt upon his back, unseemly clad, and homely to behold. He brings nets to dry and repair |
| THIRD FISHERMAN | What say you, master? |
| MASTER | Look how thou stirrest now. Come away, or I’ll fetch th’ with a wanion. |
| THIRD FISHERMAN | [[Faith]], master, I am thinking of the poor men that were cast away before us even now. |
| MASTER | Alas, poor souls, it grieved my heart to hear what pitiful cries they made to us to help them when, [[Well\|well]]-a-day, we could scarce help ourselves. |
| THIRD FISHERMAN | Nay, master, said not I as much when I saw the porpoise how he bounced and tumbled? They say they’re half fish, half flesh. A plague on them, they ne’er come but I look to be washed. Master, I marvel how the fishes live in the sea. |
| MASTER | Why, as men do a-land—the great ones eat up the little ones. I can compare our rich misers to nothing so fitly as to a whale: a plays and tumbles, driving the poor fry before him, and at last devours them all at a mouthful. Such whales have I heard on o’th’ land, who never leave gaping till they swallowed the whole parish: church, steeple, [[Bells\|bells]], and all. [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] (aside) A pretty moral. |
| THIRD FISHERMAN | But, master, if I had been the sexton, I would have been that day in the belfry. |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | Why, man? |
| THIRD FISHERMAN | Because he should have swallowed me, too, and when I had been in his belly I would have kept such a jangling of [[The Bells\|the bells]] that he should never have left till he cast [[Bells\|bells]], steeple, church, and parish up again. But if the good [[King]] [[Simonides]] were of my mind— |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | (aside) [[Simonides]]? |
| THIRD FISHERMAN | We would purge the land of these drones that rob the bee of her honey. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | (aside)<br>How from the finny subject of the sea<br>These fishers tell th’infirmities of men,<br>And from their wat’ry empire recollect<br>All that may men approve or men detect!<br>Coming forward Peace be at your labour, honest<br>fishermen. |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | Honest, good fellow? What’s that? If it be a day fits you, scratch’t out of the calendar, and nobody look after it. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | May see the sea hath cast upon your coast— |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | What a drunken knave was the sea to cast thee in our way! |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | A man, whom both the waters and the [[Wind\|wind]]<br>In that vast tennis-[[Court\|court]] hath made the ball<br>For them to play upon, entreats you pity him.<br>He asks of you that never used to beg. |
| MASTER | No, friend, cannot you beg? Here’s them in our country of Greece gets more with begging than we can do with working. |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | Canst thou catch any fishes, then? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I never practised it. |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | Nay, then thou wilt starve, sure; for here’s nothing to be got nowadays unless thou canst fish for’t. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | What I have been, I have forgot to know,<br>But what I am, want teaches me to think on:<br>A man thronged up with cold; my veins are chill,<br>And have no more of life than may suffice<br>To give my tongue that heat to crave your help,<br>Which if you shall refuse, when I am dead,<br>For that I am a man, pray see me buried.<br>He falls down |
| MASTER | Die, quotha? Now, gods forbid’t an I have a gown here! To [[Pericles]], lifting him up from the ground Come, put it on, keep thee warm. Now, afore me, a handsome fellow! Come, thou shalt go [[Home\|home]], and we’ll have flesh for holidays, fish for fasting-days, and moreo’er puddings and flapjacks, and thou shalt be welcome. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I thank you, sir. |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | Hark you, my friend, you said you could not beg? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I did but crave. |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | But crave? Then I’ll turn craver too, an so I shall scape whipping. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Why, are all your beggars whipped, then? |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | O, not all, my friend, not all; for if all your beggars were whipped I would wish no better office than to be beadle. |
| MASTER | Thine office, knave— |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | Is to draw up the other nets. I’ll go. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit with Third Fisherman |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | (aside)<br>How [[Well\|well]] this honest mirth becomes their labour! |
| *(didascalia)* | MASTER seating himself by [[Pericles]] Hark you, sir, do you know where ye are? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Not [[Well\|well]]. |
| MASTER | Why, I’ll tell you. This is called Pentapolis, and our [[King\|king]] the good [[Simonides]]. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | ’The good [[Simonides]]’ do you call him? |
| MASTER | Ay, sir, and he deserves so to be called for his peaceable reign and good [[government]]. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | He is a happy [[King\|king]], since from his subjects<br>He gains the name of good by his [[government]].<br>How far is his [[Court\|court]] distant from this shore? |
| MASTER | Marry, sir, some half a day’s [[Journey\|journey]]. And I’ll tell you, he hath a fair daughter, and tomorrow is her birthday, and there are princes and knights come from all parts of the world to joust and tourney for her [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Were but my fortunes answerable<br>To my desires I could wish to make one there. |
| MASTER | O, sir, things must be as they may, and what a man cannot get himself, he may lawfully deal for with his wife’s [[Soul\|soul]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the other two Fishermen drawing up a net |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | Help, master, help! Here’s a fish hangs in the net like a poor man’s right in the law; ’twill hardly come out.<br>Before help comes, up comes their prize<br>Ha, bots on’t, ’tis come at last, and ’tis turned to a<br>rusty armour. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | An armour, friends? I pray you let me see it.<br>(Aside) Thanks, fortune, yet that after all thy crosses<br>Thou giv‘st me somewhat to repair my losses,<br>And though it was mine own, part of my heritage<br>Which my dead father did bequeath to me<br>With this strict charge ev’n as he left his life:<br>‘Keep it, my [[Pericles]]; it hath been a shield<br>‘Twixt me and [[Death\|death]],’ and pointed to this brace,<br>‘For that it saved me, keep it. In like necessity,<br>The which the Gods forfend, the same may defend thee.’<br>It kept where I kept, I so dearly loved it,<br>Till the rough seas that spares not any man<br>Took it in rage, though calmed have giv’n’t again.<br>I thank thee for’t. My shipwreck now’s no ill,<br>Since I have here my father gave in ’s will. |
| MASTER | What mean you, sir? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | To beg of you, kind friends, this coat of worth,<br>For it was sometime target to a [[King\|king]].<br>I know it by this mark. He loved me dearly,<br>And for his sake I wish the having of it,<br>And that you’d guide me to your sov’reign’s [[Court\|court]],<br>Where with’t I may appear a gentleman.<br>And if that ever my low fortune’s better,<br>I’ll pay your bounties, till then rest your debtor. |
| MASTER | Why, wilt thou tourney for the lady? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I’ll show the virtue I have learned in arms. |
| MASTER | Why, d‘ye take it, and the gods give thee good on’t! |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | Ay, but hark you, my friend, ’twas we that made up this garment through the rough seams of the waters. There are certain condolements, certain vails. I [[Hope\|hope]], sir, if you thrive, you’ll remember from whence you had this. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Believe’t, I will.<br>By your furtherance I’m clothed in steel,<br>And spite of all the rapture of the sea<br>This [[Jewel\|jewel]] holds his building on my arm.<br>Unto thy value I will mount myself<br>Upon a courser whose delightsome steps<br>Shall make the gazer joy to see him tread.<br>Only, my friends, I yet am unprovided<br>Of a pair of bases. |
| SECOND FISHERMAN | We’ll sure provide. Thou shalt have my best gown to make thee a pair, and I’ll bring thee to the [[Court\|court]] myself. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Then [[Honour\|honour]] be but equal to my will,<br>This day I’ll rise, or else add ill to ill. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt with nets and armour |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Sc. 6 |
| *(didascalia)* | Sennet.Enter [[King]] [[Simonides]] and [[Thaisa]], with Lords in attendance, and sit on two thrones |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Are the knights ready to begin the triumph? |
| FIRST LORD | They are, my liege,<br>And stay your coming to present themselves. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Return them we are ready; and our daughter,<br>In [[Honour\|honour]] of whose birth these triumphs are,<br>Sits here like [[Beauty\|beauty]]’s [[Child\|child]], whom [[Nature\|nature]] gat<br>For men to see and, seeing, [[Wonder\|wonder]] at. Exit one |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | It pleaseth you, my father, to express<br>My commendations great, whose merit’s less. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | It’s fit it should be so, for princes are<br>A model which heav’n makes like to itself.<br>As jewels lose their glory if neglected,<br>So princes their renown, if not respected.<br>’Tis now your office, daughter, to entertain<br>The labour of each knight in his device. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | Which, to preserve mine [[Honour\|honour]], I’ll perform. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish.The first knight passes by |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | richly armed, and his page before him, bearing his device on his shield, delivers it to the Lady [[Thaisa]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Who is the first that doth prefer himself? |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | A knight of Sparta, my renowned father,<br>And the device he bears upon his shield<br>Is a black Ethiop reaching at the sun.<br>The word, Lux tua vita mihi.<br>She presents it to the [[King]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | He loves you [[Well\|well]] that holds his life of you.<br>He returns it to the page, who exits with the first knight. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish.The second knight passes by |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | richly armed, and his page before him, bearing his device on his shield, delivers it to the Lady [[Thaisa]]<br>Who is the second that presents himself? |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | A prince of Macedon, my royal father,<br>And the device he bears upon his shield<br>An armed knight that’s conquered by a lady.<br>The motto thus: Piùe per dolcezza che per forza.<br>she presents it to the [[King]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | You win him more by lenity than force.<br>He returns it to the page, who exits with the second knight. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish.The third knight passes by |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | richly armed, and his page before him, bearing his device on his shield, delivers it to the Lady [[Thaisa]]<br>And what’s the third? |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | The third of Antioch,<br>And his device a wreath of chivalry.<br>The word, Me pompae provexit apex.<br>She presents it to the [[King]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | [[Desire]] of renown he doth devise,<br>The which hath drawn him to this enterprise.<br>He returns it to the page, who exits with the third knight. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish.The fourth knight passes by |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | richly armed, and his page before him, bearing his device on his shield, delivers it to the Lady [[Thaisa]]<br>What is the fourth? |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | A knight of Athens bearing<br>A burning torch that’s turned upside down.<br>The word, Qui me alit me extinguit.<br>She presents it to the [[King]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Which shows that [[Beauty\|beauty]] hath this power and will,<br>Which can as [[Well\|well]] inflame as it can kill. |
| *(didascalia)* | He returns it to the page, who exits with the fourth knight. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. The fifth Knight passes by richly armed, and his page before him, bearing his device on his shield, delivers it to the Lady [[Thaisa]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | And who the fifth? |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | The fifth, a prince of Corinth,<br>Presents an hand environèd with clouds,<br>Holding out gold that’s by the [[Touchstone\|touchstone]] tried.<br>The motto thus: Sic spectanda fides. |
| *(didascalia)* | She presents it to the [[King]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | So [[Faith\|faith]] is to be looked into. |
| *(didascalia)* | He returns it to the page, who exits with the fifth knight. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish.l The sixth knight, [[Pericles]], in a rusty armour, who, having neither page to deliver his shield nor shield to deliver, presents his device unto the Lady [[Thaisa]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | And what’s the sixth and last, the which the knight<br>himself<br>With such a graceful courtesy delivereth? |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | He seems to be a stranger, but his present is<br>A withered branch that’s only green at top.<br>The motto, In hac spe vivo. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | From the dejected state wherein he is<br>He hopes by you his fortunes yet may flourish. |
| FIRST LORD | He had need mean better than his outward show<br>Can any way speak in his just commend,<br>For by his rusty outside he appears<br>T’have practised more the whipstock than the lance. |
| SECOND LORD | He [[Well\|well]] may be a stranger, for he comes<br>Unto an honoured triumph strangely furnished. |
| THIRD LORD | And on set purpose let his armour rust<br>Until this day, to scour it in the [[Dust\|dust]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Opinion’s but a [[Fool\|fool]], that makes us scan<br>The outward habit for the inward man. |
| *(didascalia)* | Cornetts |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | But stay, the knights are coming. We will withdraw |
| *(didascalia)* | Into the gallery. Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | Cornetts and great shouts within, and all cry |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | ‘The mean knight!’ |
| *(didascalia)* | Sc. 7 A stately banquet is brought in.1 Enter [[King]] |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Simonides]], [[Thaisa]] and their train at one door, and at another door a Marshal conducting [[Pericles]] and the other knights from tilting |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | (to the knights)<br>To say you’re welcome were superfluous.<br>To place upon the volume of your deeds<br>As in a title page your worth in arms<br>Were more than you expect, or more than’s fit,<br>Since every worth in show commends itself.<br>Prepare for mirth, for mirth becomes a feast.<br>You’re princes, and my guests. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | (to [[Pericles]]) But you, my knight and guest; To whom this wreath of victory I give, And [[Crown\|crown]] you [[King\|king]] of this day’s happiness. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | ’Tis more by fortune, lady, than my merit. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Call it by what you will, the day is yours,<br>And here I [[Hope\|hope]] is none that envies it.<br>In framing artists art hath thus decreed,<br>To make some good, but others to exceed.<br>You are her laboured scholar. (To [[Thaisa]]) Come,<br>[[Queen\|queen]] o’th’ feast—<br>For, daughter, so you are—here take your place.<br>(To Marshal) Marshal the rest as they deserve their<br>grace. |
| KNIGHTS | We are honoured much by good [[Simonides]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Your presence glads our days; [[Honour\|honour]] we [[Love\|love]],<br>For who hates [[Honour\|honour]] hates the gods above. |
| MARSHAL | (to [[Pericles]])<br>Sir, yonder is your place. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Some other is more fit. |
| FIRST KNIGHT | Contend not, sir, for we are gentlemen<br>Have neither in our hearts nor outward eyes<br>Envied the great, nor shall the low despise. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | You are right courteous knights. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Sit, Sir, sit. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Pericles]] sits directly over against the [[King]] and [[Thaisa]]. The guests feed apace. [[Pericles]] sits still and eats nothing |
| *(didascalia)* | Aside By Jove I [[Wonder\|wonder]], that is [[King\|king]] of thoughts, These cates distaste me, he but thought upon. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] aside |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | By Juno, that is [[Queen\|queen]] of [[Marriage\|marriage]],<br>I am amazed all viands that I eat<br>Do seem unsavoury, wishing him my meat. |
| *(didascalia)* | To the [[King]] Sure he’s a gallant gentleman. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | He’s but a country gentleman.<br>He’s done no more than other knights have done.<br>He’s broke a staff or so, so let it pass. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] aside |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | To me he seems like diamond to glass. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] aside |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Yon [[King\|king]]’s to me like to my father’s picture,<br>Which tells me in what glory once he was—<br>Had princes sit like [[Stars\|stars]] about his throne,<br>And he the sun for them to reverence.<br>None that beheld him but like lesser lights<br>Did vail their crowns to his supremacy;<br>Where now his son’s a glow-worm in the [[Night\|night]],<br>The which hath [[Fire\|fire]] in darkness, none in light;<br>Whereby I see that [[Time\|time]]’s the [[King\|king]] of men;<br>He’s both their parent and he is their [[Grave\|grave]],<br>And gives them what he will, not what they crave. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] What, are you merry, knights? THE OTHER KNIGHTS |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Who can be other in this royal presence? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Here with a cup that’s stored unto the brim,<br>As you do [[Love\|love]], full to your mistress’ lips,<br>We drink this health to you. |
| *(didascalia)* | THE OTHER KNIGHTS We thank your grace. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Yet pause a while. Yon knight doth sit too<br>[[Melancholy\|melancholy]],<br>As if the entertainment in our [[Court\|court]]<br>Had not a show might countervail his worth.<br>Note it not you, [[Thaisa]]? |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | What is’t to me, my father? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | O, attend, my daughter. Princes in this<br>Should live like gods above, who freely give<br>To everyone that come to [[Honour\|honour]] them.<br>And princes not so doing are like gnats<br>Which make a sound but, killed, are wondered at.<br>Therefore to make his entertain more sweet,<br>Here bear this standing-bowl of wine to him. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | Alas, my father, it befits not me<br>Unto a stranger knight to be so bold.<br>He may my proffer take for an offence,<br>Since men take women’s gifts for impudence. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | How? Do as I bid you, or you’ll move me else. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | (aside)<br>Now, by the gods, he could not please me better. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Furthermore, tell him we [[Desire\|desire]] to know<br>Of whence he is, his name and parentage. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Thaisa]] bears the cup to [[Pericles]] |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | The [[King]] my father, sir, has drunk to you,<br>Wishing it so much [[Blood\|blood]] unto your life. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I thank both him and you, and pledge him freely.<br>He pledges the [[King]] |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | And further he desires to know of you<br>Of whence you are, your name and parentage. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | A gentleman of Tyre, my name [[Pericles]],<br>My [[Education\|education]] been in arts and arms,<br>Who, looking for adventures in the world,<br>Was by the rough unconstant seas bereft<br>Unfortunately both of ships and men,<br>And after shipwreck driven upon this shore. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Thaisa]] returns to the [[King]] |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | He thanks your grace, names himself [[Pericles]],<br>A gentleman of Tyre, who, seeking adventures,<br>Was solely by misfortune of the seas<br>Bereft of ships and men, cast on this shore. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Now by the gods I pity his mishaps,<br>And will awake him from his [[Melancholy\|melancholy]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Simonides]], rising from his state, goes forthwith and embraces [[Pericles]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Be cheered, for what misfortune hath impaired you of,<br>Fortune by my help can repair to you.<br>My [[Self\|self]] and country both shall be your friends,<br>And presently a goodly milk-white steed<br>And golden spurs I first bestow upon you,<br>The prizes due your merit, and ordained<br>For this day’s enterprise. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Your kingly courtesy I thankfully accept. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Come, gentlemen, we sit too long on trifles,<br>And waste the [[Time\|time]] which looks for other revels.<br>Ev’n in your armours, as you are addressed,<br>Your limbs will [[Well\|well]] become a soldier’s [[Dance\|dance]].<br>I will not have excuse with saying this,<br>‘Loud music is too harsh for ladies’ heads’,<br>Since they [[Love\|love]] men in arms as [[Well\|well]] as beds.<br>The knights [[Dance\|dance]]<br>So this was [[Well\|well]] asked, ’twas so [[Well\|well]] performed.<br>Come, here’s a lady that wants breathing too.<br>(To [[Pericles]]) And I have heard, sir, that the knights of<br>Tyre<br>Are excellent in making ladies trip,<br>And that their measures are as excellent. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | In those that practise them they are, my lord. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | O, that’s as much as you would be denied<br>Of your fair courtesy. Unclasp, unclasp.<br>They [[Dance\|dance]]<br>Thanks, gentlemen, to all. All have done [[Well\|well]],<br>(To [[Pericles]]) But you the best.—Lights, pages, to<br>conduct<br>These knights unto their sev’ral lodgings.—Yours, sir,<br>We have giv’n order should be next our own. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I am at your grace’s pleasure. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Princes, it is too late to talk of [[Love\|love]],<br>And that’s the mark I know you level at.<br>Therefore each one betake him to his rest;<br>Tomorrow all for speeding do their best. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Sc. 8 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Helicanus and Aeschines |
| HELICANUS | No, Aeschines, know this of me:<br>[[Antiochus]] from incest lived not free,<br>For which the most high gods, not minding longer<br>To hold the vengeance that they had in store<br>Due to this heinous capital offence,<br>Even in the height and [[Pride\|pride]] of all his glory,<br>When he was seated in a chariot<br>Of an inestimable value, and<br>His daughter with him, both apparelled all in jewels,<br>A [[Fire\|fire]] from heaven came and shrivelled up<br>Their bodies e’en to loathing, for they so stunk<br>That all those eyes adored them ere their fall<br>Scorn now their hands should give them burial. |
| AESCHINES | ’Twas very strange. |
| HELICANUS | And yet but justice, for though<br>This [[King\|king]] were great, his [[Greatness\|greatness]] was no guard To bar heav’n’s shaft, but sin had his reward. |
| AESCHINES | ’Tis very true. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter three Lords, and stand aside |
| FIRST LORD | See, not a man in private conference<br>Or council has respect with him but he. |
| SECOND LORD | It shall no longer grieve without reproof. |
| THIRD LORD | And cursed be he that will not second it. |
| FIRST LORD | Follow me, then.—Lord Helicane, a word. |
| HELICANUS | With me? And welcome. Happy day, my lords. |
| FIRST LORD | Know that our griefs are risen to the top,<br>And now at length they overflow their banks. |
| HELICANUS | Your griefs? For what? Wrong not your prince you [[Love\|love]]. |
| FIRST LORD | Wrong not yourself, then, noble Helicane,<br>But if the prince do live, let us salute him<br>Or know what ground’s made happy by his step,<br>And be resolved he lives to govern us,<br>Or dead, give ’s cause to mourn his funeral<br>And leave us to our free election. |
| SECOND LORD | Whose [[Death\|death]] indeed’s the strongest in our censure,<br>And knowing this—kingdoms without a head,<br>Like goodly buildings left without a roof,<br>Soon fall to utter ruin—your noble [[Self\|self]],<br>That best know how to rule and how to reign,<br>We thus submit unto as sovereign. |
| *(didascalia)* | ALL kneeling Live, noble Helicane! |
| HELICANUS | By [[Honour\|honour]]’s cause, forbear your suffrages.<br>If that you [[Love\|love]] Prince [[Pericles]], forbear. |
| *(didascalia)* | The lords rise |
| HELICANUS | Take I your wish I leap into the seas<br>Where’s hourly trouble for a minute’s ease,<br>But if I cannot win you to this [[Love\|love]],<br>A twelvemonth longer then let me entreat you<br>Further to bear the absence of your [[King\|king]];<br>If in which [[Time\|time]] expired he not return,<br>I shall with aged patience bear your yoke.<br>Go, seek your noble prince like noble subjects,<br>And in your search spend your adventurous worth,<br>Whom if you find and win unto return,<br>You shall like diamonds sit about his [[Crown\|crown]]. |
| FIRST LORD | To wisdom he’s a [[Fool\|fool]] that will not yield,<br>And since Lord Helicane enjoineth us,<br>We with our travels will endeavour us.<br>If in the world he live we’ll seek him out;<br>If in his [[Grave\|grave]] he rest, we’ll find him there. |
| HELICANUS | Then you [[Love\|love]] us, we you, and we’ll clasp hands.<br>When peers thus knit, a kingdom ever stands. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 8a |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Pericles]] with Gentlemen with lights |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Here is your lodging, sir. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Pray leave me private.<br>Only for instant solace pleasure me<br>With some delightful instrument, with which,<br>And with my former practice, I intend<br>To pass away the tediousness of [[Night\|night]], 5<br>Though slumbers were more fitting. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Presently. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit First Gentleman |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Your will’s obeyed in all things, for our master<br>Commanded you be disobeyed in nothing. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter First Gentleman with a stringed instrument |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I thank you. Now betake you to your pillows,<br>And to the nourishment of quiet [[Sleep\|sleep]]. 10 |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Gentlemen |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | [[Pericles]] plays and sings<br>Day—that hath still that sovereignty to draw back<br>The empire of the [[Night\|night]], though for a while<br>In darkness she usurp—brings morning on.<br>I will go give his grace that salutation<br>Morning requires of me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit with instrument |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Sc. 9 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Simonides]] at one door reading of a letter, the Knights enter at another door and meet him |
| FIRST KNIGHT | Good morrow to the good [[Simonides]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Knights, from my daughter this I let you know:<br>That for this twelvemonth she’ll not undertake<br>A married life. Her reason to herself<br>Is only known, which from her none can get. |
| SECOND KNIGHT | May we not have access to her, my lord? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | [[Faith]], by no means. It is impossible,<br>She hath so strictly tied her to her chamber.<br>One twelve moons more she’ll wear [[Diana]]’s liv’ry.<br>This by the eye of [[Cynthia]] hath she vowed,<br>And on her virgin [[Honour\|honour]] will not break it. |
| THIRD KNIGHT | Loath to bid farewell, we take our [[Leaves\|leaves]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Knights |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | So, they are [[Well\|well]] dispatched. Now to my daughter’s<br>letter.<br>She tells me here she’ll wed the stranger knight,<br>Or never more to view nor day nor light.<br>I like that [[Well\|well]]. Nay, how absolute she’s in‘t,<br>Not minding whether I dislike or no!<br>Mistress, ’tis [[Well\|well]], I do commend your choice,<br>And will no longer have it be delayed. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Pericles]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Soft, here he comes. I must dissemble that<br>In show, I have determined on in heart. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | All fortune to the good [[Simonides]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | To you as much, sir. I am beholden to you<br>For your sweet music this last [[Night\|night]]. My ears,<br>I do protest, were never better fed<br>With such delightful pleasing harmony. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | It is your grace’s pleasure to commend,<br>Not my [[Desert\|desert]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Sir, you are music’s master. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | The worst of all her scholars, my good lord. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Let me ask you one thing. What think you of my daughter? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | A most virtuous princess. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | And fair, too, is she not? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | As a fair day in [[Summer\|summer]]; wondrous fair. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | My daughter, sir, thinks very [[Well\|well]] of you;<br>So [[Well\|well]] indeed that you must be her master<br>And she will be your scholar; therefore look to it. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I am unworthy for her schoolmaster. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | She thinks not so. Peruse this writing else.<br>He gives the letter to [[Pericles]], who reads |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | (aside)<br>What’s here?—a letter that she loves the knight of Tyre?<br>’Tis the [[King]]’s subtlety to have my life. |
| *(didascalia)* | He prostrates himself at the [[King]]’s feet |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | O, seek not to entrap me, gracious lord,<br>A stranger and distressed gentleman<br>That never aimed so high to [[Love\|love]] your daughter,<br>But bent all offices to [[Honour\|honour]] her.<br>Never did thought of mine levy offence,<br>Nor never did my actions yet commence<br>A deed might gain her [[Love\|love]] or your displeasure. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Thou liest like a traitor. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Traitor? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Ay, traitor,<br>That thus disguised art stol’n into my [[Court\|court]]<br>With witchcraft of thy actions to bewitch<br>The yielding spirit of my tender [[Child\|child]]. 50 |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] rising |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Who calls me traitor, unless it be the [[King]],<br>Ev’n in his bosom I will write the lie. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | (aside)<br>Now, by the gods, I do applaud his [[Courage\|courage]]. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | My actions are as noble as my [[Blood\|blood]],<br>That never relished of a base descent. 55<br>I came unto your [[Court\|court]] in search of [[Honour\|honour]],<br>And not to be a [[Rebel\|rebel]] to your state;<br>And he that otherwise accounts of me,<br>This [[Sword\|sword]] shall prove he’s [[Honour\|honour]]’s enemy. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | I shall prove otherwise, since both your practice<br>And her consent therein is evident<br>There, by my daughter’s hand, as she can witness. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Thaisa]] |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | (to [[Thaisa]])<br>Then as you are as virtuous as fair,<br>By what you [[Hope\|hope]] of heaven or [[Desire\|desire]]<br>By your best wishes here i‘th’ world fulfilled,<br>Resolve your angry father if my tongue<br>Did e’er solicit, or my hand subscribe<br>To any syllable made [[Love\|love]] to you. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | Why, sir, say if you had,<br>Who takes offence at that would make me glad? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | How, minion, are you so peremptory?<br>(Aside) I am glad on’t.—Is this a fit match for you?<br>A straggling [[Theseus]], born we know not where,<br>One that hath neither [[Blood\|blood]] nor merit<br>For thee to [[Hope\|hope]] for, or himself to challenge<br>Of thy perfections e’en the least allowance. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | (kneeling)<br>Suppose his birth were base, when that his life<br>Shows that he is not so, yet he hath virtue,<br>The very ground of all nobility,<br>Enough to make him noble. I entreat you<br>To remember that I am in [[Love\|love]],<br>The power of which [[Love\|love]] cannot be confined<br>By th’ power of your will. Most royal father,<br>What with my pen I have in secret written<br>With my tongue now I openly confirm,<br>Which is I have no life but in his [[Love\|love]],<br>Nor any being but in joying of his worth. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Equals to equals, good to good is joined.<br>This not being so, the bavin of your mind<br>In rashness kindled must again be quenched,<br>Or purchase our displeasure.—And for you, sir,<br>First learn to know I banish you my [[Court\|court]],<br>And yet I scorn our rage should stoop so low.<br>For your [[Ambition\|ambition]], sir, I’ll have your life. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | (to [[Pericles]])<br>For every drop of [[Blood\|blood]] he sheds of yours<br>He’ll draw another from his only [[Child\|child]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | I’ll tame you, yea, I’ll bring you in subjection.<br>Will you not having my consent<br>Bestow your [[Love\|love]] and your affections<br>Upon a stranger?—(aside) who for aught I know<br>May be, nor can I think the contrary,<br>As great in [[Blood\|blood]] as I myself. |
| *(didascalia)* | He catches [[Thaisa]] rashly by the hand |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Therefore hear you, mistress: either frame your will to<br>mine— |
| *(didascalia)* | He catches [[Pericles]] rashly by the hand |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | And you, sir, hear you: either be ruled by me—<br>Or I shall make you |
| *(didascalia)* | He claps their hands together man and wife. 105 |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | Nay, come, your hands and lips must seal it too,<br>[[Pericles]] and [[Thaisa]] kiss<br>And being joined, I’ll thus your hopes destroy, |
| *(didascalia)* | He parts them |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | And for your further grief, God give you joy. What, are you pleased? |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | Yes, (to [[Pericles]]) if you [[Love\|love]] me, sir. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Ev’n as my life my [[Blood\|blood]] that fosters it. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | What, are you both agreed? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | and [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] Yes, if’t please your majesty. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Simonides\|SIMONIDES]] | It pleaseth me so [[Well\|well]] that I will see you wed,<br>Then with what haste you can, get you to bed. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 10 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Gower]] |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | Now [[Sleep\|sleep]] y-slacked hath the rout,<br>No din but snores the [[house]] about,<br>Made louder by the o‘erfed breast<br>Of this most pompous [[Marriage\|marriage]] feast.<br>The cat with eyne of burning coal<br>Now couches fore the mouse’s hole,<br>And crickets sing at th’oven’s mouth<br>As the blither for their drouth.<br>Hymen hath brought the bride to bed,<br>Where by the loss of maidenhead<br>A babe is moulded. Be attent,<br>And [[Time\|time]] that is so briefly spent<br>With your fine fancies quaintly eche.<br>What’s dumb in show, I’ll plain with speech.<br>Dumb show. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Pericles]] and [[Simonides]] at one door with attendants. A messenger comes hastily in to them, kneels, and gives [[Pericles]] a letter. [[Pericles]] shows it [[Simonides]]; the lords kneel to him. Then enter [[Thaisa]] with [[Child\|child]], with Lychorida, a [[Nurse\|nurse]]. The [[King]] shows her the letter. She rejoices. She and [[Pericles]] take leave of her father and depart with I,,ychorida at one door; [[Simonides]] and attendants depart at another |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | By many a dern and painful perch<br>Of [[Pericles]] the care-full search,<br>By the four opposing coigns<br>Which the world together joins,<br>Is made with all due diligence<br>That [[Horse\|horse]] and sail and high expense<br>Can stead the [[Quest\|quest]]. At last from Tyre<br>Fame answering the most strange enquire,<br>To th’ [[Court\|court]] of [[King]] [[Simonides]]<br>Are letters brought, the tenor these:<br>[[Antiochus]] and his daughter dead,<br>The men of Tyrus on the head<br>Of Helicanus would set on<br>The [[Crown\|crown]] of Tyre, but he will none.<br>The mutiny there he hastes t‘appease,<br>Says to ’em if [[King]] [[Pericles]]<br>Come not [[Home\|home]] in twice six moons<br>He, obedient to their dooms,<br>Will take the [[Crown\|crown]]. The sum of this<br>Brought hither to Pentapolis<br>Y-ravishèd the regions round,<br>And everyone with claps can sound<br>‘Our heir-apparent is a [[King\|king]]!<br>Who dreamt, who thought of such a thing?’<br>Brief he must hence depart to Tyre;<br>His [[Queen\|queen]] with [[Child\|child]] makes her [[Desire\|desire]]—<br>Which who shall [[Cross\|cross]]?—along to go.<br>Omit we all their dole and woe.<br>Lychorida her [[Nurse\|nurse]] she takes,<br>And so to sea. Their vessel shakes<br>On Neptune’s billow. Half the flood<br>Hath their keel cut, but fortune’s mood<br>Varies again. The grizzled north<br>Disgorges such a tempest forth<br>That as a duck for life that dives,<br>So up and down the poor [[Ship\|ship]] drives.<br>The lady shrieks, and [[Well\|well]]-a-near<br>Does fall in travail with her [[Fear\|fear]],<br>And what ensues in this fell [[Storm\|storm]]<br>Shall for itself itself perform;<br>I nill relate; action may<br>Conveniently the rest convey,<br>Which might not what by me is told.<br>In your [[Imagination\|imagination]] hold<br>This stage the [[Ship\|ship]], upon whose deck<br>The sea-tossed [[Pericles]] appears to speke. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Sc. 11 Thunder and [[Lightning\|lightning]]. Enter [[Pericles]] a-shipboard |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | The god of this great vast rebuke these surges<br>Which wash both heav’n and hell; and thou that hast<br>Upon the winds command, bind them in brass,<br>Having called them from the deep. O still<br>Thy deaf‘ning dreadful thunders, gently quench<br>Thy nimble sulph’rous flashes.—O, ho, Lychorida!<br>How does my [[Queen\|queen]]?—Thou stormest venomously.<br>Wilt thou spit all thyself The seaman’s whistle<br>Is as a whisper in the ears of [[Death\|death]],<br>Unheard.—Lychorida!—Lucina, O!<br>Divinest patroness, and midwife gentle<br>To those that cry by [[Night\|night]], convey thy deity<br>Aboard our dancing boat, make swift the pangs<br>Of my [[Queen\|queen]]’s travails!—Now, Lychorida. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lychorida with an infant |
| LYCHORIDA | Here is a thing too young for such a place,<br>Who, if it had conceit, would die, as I<br>Am like to do. Take in your arms this piece<br>Of your dead [[Queen\|queen]]. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | How, how, Lychorida? |
| LYCHORIDA | Patience, good sir, do not assist the [[Storm\|storm]].<br>Here’s all that is left living of your [[Queen\|queen]],<br>A little daughter. For the sake of it<br>Be manly, and take comfort. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | O you gods!<br>Why do you make us [[Love\|love]] your goodly gifts,<br>And snatch them straight away? We here below<br>Recall not what we give, and therein may<br>Use [[Honour\|honour]] with you. |
| LYCHORIDA | Patience, good sir,<br>E’en for this charge. |
| *(didascalia)* | She gives him the infant. [[Pericles]], looking mournfully upon it, shakes his head, and weeps |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Now mild may be thy life,<br>For a more blust‘rous birth had never babe;<br>Quiet and gentle thy conditions, for<br>Thou art the rudeliest welcome to this world<br>That e’er was prince’s [[Child\|child]]; happy what follows.<br>Thou hast as chiding a nativity<br>As [[Fire\|fire]], air, [[Water\|water]], earth, and heav’n can make<br>To herald thee from th’ womb. Poor inch of [[Nature\|nature]],<br>Ev’n at the first thy loss is more than can<br>Thy partage quit with all thou canst find here.<br>Now the good gods throw their best eyes upon’t. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Master and a Sailor |
| *(didascalia)* | MASTER What, [[Courage\|courage]], sir! God save you. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | [[Courage]] enough, I do not [[Fear\|fear]] the flaw;<br>It hath done to me its worst. Yet for the [[Love\|love]]<br>Of this poor infant, this fresh new seafarer,<br>I would it would be quiet. |
| *(didascalia)* | MASTER (calling) Slack the bow-lines, there.—Thou wilt not, wilt thou? Blow, and split thyself. |
| SAILOR | But searoom, an the brine and cloudy billow kiss the [[Moon\|moon]], I care not. |
| *(didascalia)* | MASTER (to [[Pericles]]) Sir, your [[Queen\|queen]] must overboard. The sea works high, the [[Wind\|wind]] is loud, and will not lie till the [[Ship\|ship]] be cleared of the dead. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | That’s but your superstition. |
