| *(didascalia)* | MASTER Pardon us, sir; with us at sea it hath been still observed, and we are strong in custom. Therefore briefly yield ’er, for she must overboard straight. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | As you think meet. Most wretched [[Queen\|queen]]! |
| LYCHORIDA | Here she lies, sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | She draws the curtains and discovers the [[Body\|body]] of [[Thaisa]] in a bed. [[Pericles]] gives Lychorida the infant |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | (to [[Thaisa]])<br>A terrible childbed hast thou had, my dear,<br>No light, no [[Fire\|fire]]. Th‘unfriendly elements<br>Forgot thee utterly, nor have I [[Time\|time]]<br>To give thee hallowed to thy [[Grave\|grave]], but straight<br>Must cast thee, scarcely coffined, in the ooze,<br>Where, for a monument upon thy bones<br>And aye-remaining lamps, the belching whale<br>And humming [[Water\|water]] must o’erwhelm thy corpse,<br>Lying with simple shetts.—O Lychorida,<br>Bid Nestor bring me spices, ink, and paper,<br>My casket and my jewels, and bid Nicander<br>Bring me the satin coffer. Lay the babe<br>Upon the pillow. Hie thee whiles I say<br>A priestly farewell to her. Suddenly, woman. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Lychorida |
| *(didascalia)* | SAILOR Sir, we have a chest beneath the hatches caulked and bitumed ready. |
| *(didascalia)* | I thank thee. To the Master Mariner, say, what coast is this? |
| *(didascalia)* | MASTER |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | We are near Tarsus. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Thither, gentle mariner, |
| *(didascalia)* | Alter thy course from Tyre. When canst thou reach it? MASTER |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | By break of day, if the [[Wind\|wind]] cease. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Make for Tarsus.<br>There will I visit [[Cleon]], for the babe<br>Cannot hold out to Tyrus. There I’ll leave it<br>At careful nursing. Go thy ways, good mariner.<br>I’ll bring the [[Body\|body]] presently. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Master at one door and Sailor beneath the hatches. Exit [[Pericles]] to [[Thaisa]], closing the curtains |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Sc. 12 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lord Cerimon with a poor man and a servant |
| CERIMON | Philemon, ho! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Philemon |
| PHILEMON | Doth my lord call? |
| CERIMON | Get [[Fire\|fire]] and meat for those poor men. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Philemon |
| CERIMON | ‘T’as been a turbulent and stormy [[Night\|night]]. |
| SERVANT | I have seen many, but such a [[Night\|night]] as this<br>Till now I ne’er endured. |
| CERIMON | Your master will be dead ere you return.<br>There’s nothing can be ministered in [[Nature\|nature]] |
| *(didascalia)* | That can recover him. To poor man Give this to th’ |
| CERIMON | pothecary<br>And tell me how it works. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt poor man and servant |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter two Gentlemen |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Good morrow. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Good morrow to your lordship. |
| CERIMON | Gentlemen,<br>Why do you stir so early? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Sir,<br>Our lodgings, standing bleak upon the sea,<br>Shook as the earth did quake.<br>The very principals did seem to rend<br>And all to topple. Pure surprise and [[Fear\|fear]]<br>Made me to quit the [[house]]. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | That is the cause we trouble you so early;<br>’Tis not our husbandry. |
| CERIMON | O, you say [[Well\|well]]. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | But I much marvel that your lordship should,<br>Having rich tire about you, at this hour<br>Shake off the golden slumber of repose. ’Tis most<br>strange,<br>[[Nature]] to be so conversant with [[Pain\|pain]],<br>Being thereto not compelled. |
| CERIMON | I held it ever<br>Virtue and cunning were endowments greater<br>Than nobleness and riches. Careless heirs<br>May the two latter darken and dispend,<br>But [[Immortality\|immortality]] attends the former,<br>Making a man a god. ‘Tis known I ever<br>Have studied physic, through which secret art,<br>By turning o’er authorities, I have,<br>Together with my practice, made familiar<br>To me and to my aid the blest infusions<br>That dwells in vegetives, in metals, stones,<br>And so can speak of the disturbances<br>That [[Nature\|nature]] works, and of her cures, which doth<br>give me<br>A more content and cause of true delight<br>Than to be thirsty after tott’ring [[Honour\|honour]],<br>Or tie my pleasure up in silken bags<br>To glad the [[Fool\|fool]] and [[Death\|death]]. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Your [[Honour\|honour]] has<br>Through Ephesus poured forth your [[Charity\|charity]],<br>And hundreds call themselves your creatures who by<br>you<br>Have been restored. And not [[Alone\|alone]] your knowledge,<br>Your personal [[Pain\|pain]], but e’en your purse still open<br>Hath built Lord Cerimon such strong renown<br>As [[Time\|time]] shall never— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Philemon and one or two with a chest |
| *(didascalia)* | PHILEMON So, lift there. |
| *(didascalia)* | CERIMON What’s that? PHILEMON Sir, even now |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | The sea tossed up upon our shore this chest.<br>’Tis off some wreck. |
| CERIMON | Set’t down. Let’s look upon’t. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | ’Tis like a coffin, sir. |
| CERIMON | Whate’er it be,<br>’Tis wondrous heavy.—Did the sea cast it up? |
| *(didascalia)* | PHILEMON |
| CERIMON | I never saw so huge a billow, sir,<br>Or a more eager. |
| CERIMON | Wrench it open straight.<br>The others start to work<br>If the sea’s stomach be o‘ercharged with gold<br>’Tis by a good constraint of queasy fortune<br>It belches upon us. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | ’Tis so, my lord. |
| CERIMON | How close ’tis caulked and bitumed! |
| *(didascalia)* | They force the lid |
| CERIMON | Soft, it smells<br>Most sweetly in my sense. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | A delicate odour. |
| CERIMON | As ever hit my nostril. So, up with it.<br>They take the lid off<br>O you most potent gods! What’s here—a corpse? |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Most strange. |
| CERIMON | Shrouded in cloth of state, and crowned,<br>Balmed and entreasured with full bags of spices.<br>A passport, too!<br>He takes a paper from the chest<br>[[Apollo]] perfect me i’th’ characters.<br>‘Here I give to understand,<br>If e’er this coffin drives a-land,<br>I, [[King]] [[Pericles]], have lost<br>This [[Queen\|queen]] worth all our mundane cost.<br>Who finds her, give her burying;<br>She was the daughter of a [[King\|king]].<br>Besides this treasure for a fee,<br>The gods requite his [[Charity\|charity]].’<br>If thou liv’st, [[Pericles]], thou hast a heart<br>That even cracks for woe. This chanced tonight. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Most likely, sir. |
| CERIMON | Nay, certainly tonight,<br>For look how fresh she looks. They were too rash<br>That threw her in the sea. Make a [[Fire\|fire]] within. |
| *(didascalia)* | Fetch hither all my boxes in my closet. Exit Philemon |
| CERIMON | [[Death]] may usurp on [[Nature\|nature]] many hours,<br>And yet the [[Fire\|fire]] of life kindle again<br>The o’erpressed spirits. I have heard<br>Of an Egyptian nine hours dead<br>Who was by good appliances recovered. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Philemon with napkins and [[Fire\|fire]] |
| CERIMON | [[Well]] said, [[Well\|well]] said, the [[Fire\|fire]] and cloths.<br><br>The still and woeful music that we have,<br>Cause it to sound, beseech you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Music |
| CERIMON | The vial once more.<br>How thou stirr‘st, thou block! The music there!<br>I pray you give her air. Gentlemen,<br>This [[Queen\|queen]] will live. [[Nature]] awakes, a warmth<br>Breathes out of her. She hath not been entranced<br>Above five hours. See how she ’gins to blow<br>Into life’s flow’r again. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | The heavens<br>Through you increase our [[Wonder\|wonder]], and set up<br>Your fame for ever. |
| CERIMON | She is alive. Behold,<br>Her eyelids, cases to those heav’nly jewels<br>Which [[Pericles]] hath lost,<br>Begin to part their fringes of bright gold.<br>The diamonds of a most praised [[Water\|water]]<br>Doth appear to make the world twice rich.—Live,<br>And make us weep to hear your fate, fair creature,<br>Rare as you seem to be.<br>She moves |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | O dear [[Diana]],<br>Where am I? Where’s my lord? What world is this? |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Is not this strange? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Most rare. |
| CERIMON | Hush, gentle neighbours. Lend me your hands. To the next chamber bear her. Get linen. Now this matter must be looked to, For her relapse is mortal. Come, come, And Aesculapius guide us. They carry her away. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 13 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Pericles]] at Tarsus, with [[Cleon]] and [[Dionyza]], and Lychorida with a babe |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Most honoured [[Cleon]], I must needs be gone.<br>My twelve months are expired, and Tyrus stands<br>In a litigious peace. You and your lady<br>Take from my heart all thankfulness. The gods<br>Make up the rest upon you! |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | Your strokes of fortune, Though they hurt you mortally, yet glance Full woundingly on us. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | O your sweet [[Queen\|queen]]!<br>That the strict fates had pleased you’d brought her<br>hither<br>T’have blessed mine eyes with her! |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | We cannot but obey<br>The pow‘rs above us. Should I rage and roar<br>As doth the sea she lies in, yet the end<br>Must be as ’tis. My gentle babe [[Marina]],<br>Whom for she was born at sea I have named so,<br>Here I charge your [[Charity\|charity]] withal, and leave her<br>The infant of your care, beseeching you<br>To give her princely training, that she may be<br>Mannered as she is born. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | [[Fear]] not, my lord, but think<br>Your grace, that fed my country with your corn—<br>For which the people’s pray’rs still fall upon you—<br>Must in your [[Child\|child]] be thought on. If neglection<br>Should therein make me vile, the common [[Body\|body]]<br>By you relieved would force me to my duty.<br>But if to that my [[Nature\|nature]] need a spur,<br>The gods [[Revenge\|revenge]] it upon me and mine<br>To th’ end of generation. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I believe you.<br>Your [[Honour\|honour]] and your goodness teach me to’t<br>Without your vows.—Till she be married, madam,<br>By bright [[Diana]], whom we [[Honour\|honour]] all,<br>Unscissored shall this hair of mine remain,<br>Though I show ill in’t. So I take my leave.<br>Good madam, make me blessed in your care<br>In bringing up my [[Child\|child]]. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | I have one myself,<br>Who shall not be more dear to my respect<br>Than yours, my lord. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Madam, my thanks and prayers. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | We’ll bring your grace e‘en to the edge o’th’ shore,<br>Then give you up to th’ masted Neptune and<br>The gentlest winds of heaven. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I will embrace your offer.—Come, dear’st madam.—<br>O, no [[Tears\|tears]], Lychorida, no [[Tears\|tears]].<br>Look to your little mistress, on whose grace<br>You may depend hereafter.—Come, my lord. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 14 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Cerimon and [[Thaisa]] |
| CERIMON | Madam, this letter and some certain jewels<br>Lay with you in your coffer, which are all<br>At your command. Know you the character? |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | It is my lord’s. That I was shipped at sea<br>I [[Well\|well]] remember, ev’n on my eaning [[Time\|time]],<br>But whether there delivered, by th’ holy gods<br>I cannot rightly say. But since [[King]] [[Pericles]],<br>My wedded lord, I ne‘er shall see again,<br>A vestal liv’ry will I take me to,<br>And never more have joy. |
| CERIMON | Madam, if this you purpose as ye speak,<br>[[Diana]]’s temple is not distant far,<br>Where till your date expire you may abide.<br>Moreover, if you please a niece of mine<br>Shall there attend you. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | My recompense is thanks, that’s all,<br>Yet my good will is great, though the gift small. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 15 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Gower]] |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | Imagine [[Pericles]] arrived at Tyre,<br>Welcomed and settled to his own [[Desire\|desire]].<br>His woeful [[Queen\|queen]] we leave at Ephesus,<br>Unto [[Diana]] there ’s a votaress.<br>Now to [[Marina]] bend your mind,<br>Whom our fast-growing scene must find<br>At Tarsus, and by [[Cleon]] trained<br>In music, letters; who hath gained<br>Of [[Education\|education]] all the grace,<br>Which makes her both the heart and place<br>Of gen‘ral [[Wonder\|wonder]]. But, alack,<br>That [[Monster\|monster]] envy, oft the wrack<br>Of earned praise, [[Marina]]’s life<br>Seeks to take off by treason’s knife,<br>And in this kind our [[Cleon]] has<br>One daughter, and a full-grown lass<br>E’en ripe for [[Marriage\|marriage]]-rite. This maid<br>Hight Philoten, and it is said<br>For certain in our story she<br>Would ever with [[Marina]] be,<br>Be’t when they weaved the sleided silk<br>With fingers long, small, white as milk;<br>Or when she would with sharp nee‘le wound<br>The cambric which she made more sound<br>By hurting it, or when to th’ lute<br>She sung, and made the [[Night\|night]] bird mute,<br>That still records with moan; or when<br>She would with rich and constant pen<br>Vail to her mistress Dian. Still<br>This Philoten contends in skill<br>With absolute [[Marina]]; so<br>With dove of Paphos might the crow<br>Vie feathers white. [[Marina]] gets<br>All praises which are paid as debts,<br>And not as given. This so darks<br>In Philoten all graceful marks<br>That [[Cleon]]’s wife with envy rare<br>A present murder does prepare<br>For good [[Marina]], that her daughter<br>Might stand peerless by this slaughter.<br>The sooner her vile thoughts to stead<br>Lychorida, our [[Nurse\|nurse]], is dead, |
| *(didascalia)* | A tomb is revealed |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | And cursed [[Dionyza]] hath<br>The pregnant instrument of wrath<br>Pressed for this blow. Th’[[unborn]] event<br>I do commend to your content,<br>Only I carry winged [[Time]]<br>Post on the lame feet of my rhyme,<br>Which never could I so convey<br>Unless your thoughts went on my way. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Dionyza]] with Leonine |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | [[Dionyza]] does appear,<br>With Leonine, a murderer. Exit |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | Thy oath remember. Thou hast sworn to do’t.<br>‘Tis but a blow, which never shall be known.<br>Thou canst not do a thing i’th’ world so soon<br>To yield thee so much profit. Let not [[Conscience\|conscience]],<br>Which is but cold, or fanning [[Love\|love]] thy bosom<br>Unflame too nicely, nor let pity, which<br>E’en women have cast off, melt thee; but be<br>A soldier to thy purpose. |
| LEONINE | I will do’t;<br>But yet she is a goodly creature. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | The fitter then the gods should have her. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Marina]] to the tomb with a basket of [[Flowers\|flowers]] |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | Here she comes, weeping her only [[Nurse\|nurse]]’s [[Death\|death]].<br>Thou art resolved. |
| LEONINE | I am resolved. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | No, I will rob Tellus of her weed<br>To strew thy [[Grave\|grave]] with flow’rs. The yellows, blues,<br>The purple violets and marigolds<br>Shall as a carpet hang upon thy tomb<br>While [[Summer\|summer]] days doth last. Ay me, poor maid,<br>Born in a tempest when my mother died,<br>This world to me is but a ceaseless [[Storm\|storm]]<br>Whirring me from my friends. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | How now, [[Marina]], why do you keep [[Alone\|alone]]?<br>How chance my daughter is not with you?<br>Do not consume your [[Blood\|blood]] with sorrowing.<br>Have you a [[Nurse\|nurse]] of me. Lord, how your favour<br>Is changed with this unprofitable woe!<br>Give me your [[Flowers\|flowers]]. Come, o’er the sea margin<br>Walk with Leonine. The air is piercing there,<br>And quick; it sharps the stomach. Come, Leonine,<br>Take her by th’ arm. Walk with her. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | No, I pray you,<br>I’ll not bereave you of your servant. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | Come, come,<br>I [[Love\|love]] the [[King]] your father and yourself<br>With more than foreign heart. We ev’ry day<br>Expect him here. When he shall come and find<br>Our paragon to all reports thus blasted,<br>He will repent the breadth of his great voyage,<br>Blame both my lord and me, that we have taken<br>No care to your best courses. Go, I pray you,<br>Walk and be cheerful once again; resume<br>That excellent complexion which did steal<br>The eyes of young and old. Care not for me.<br>I can go [[Home\|home]] [[Alone\|alone]]. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | [[Well]], I will go,<br>But truly I have no [[Desire\|desire]] to it. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | Nay, I know ’tis good for you. Walk half an hour,<br>Leonine, at the least; remember<br>What I have said. |
| LEONINE | I warr’nt you, madam. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | (to [[Marina]])<br>I’ll leave you, my sweet lady, for a while.<br>Pray you walk softly, do not heat your [[Blood\|blood]].<br>What, I must have care of you! |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | My thanks, sweet madam. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Dionyza]] |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Is this [[Wind\|wind]] westerly that blows? |
| LEONINE | South-west. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | When I was born the [[Wind\|wind]] was north. |
| LEONINE | Was’t SO? [[Marina\|MARINA]]<br>My father, as [[Nurse\|nurse]] says, did never [[Fear\|fear]],<br>But cried ’Good seamen’ to the mariners,<br>Galling his kingly hands with haling ropes,<br>And, clasping to the mast, endured a sea<br>That almost burst the deck. |
| LEONINE | When was this? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | When I was born.<br>Never was waves nor [[Wind\|wind]] more violent.<br>Once from the ladder tackle washes off<br>A canvas-climber. ‘Ha!’ says one, ‘wolt out?’<br>And with a dropping industry they skip<br>From stem to stern. The boatswain whistles, and<br>The master calls and trebles their confusion. |
| LEONINE | Come, say your prayers. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | What mean you? |
| LEONINE | If you require a little space for [[Prayer\|prayer]]<br>I grant it. Pray, but be not tedious.<br>The gods are quick of ear, and I am sworn<br>To do my work with haste. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Why would you kill me? |
| LEONINE | To satisfy my lady. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Why would she have me killed? Now, as I can remember, by my troth<br>I never did her hurt in all my life.<br>I never spake bad word, nor did ill turn<br>To any living creature. Believe me, la.<br>I never killed a mouse nor hurt a fly.<br>I trod once on a worm against my will,<br>But I wept for it. How have I offended<br>Wherein my [[Death\|death]] might yield her any profit<br>Or my life imply her danger? |
| LEONINE | My commission<br>Is not to reason of the deed, but do’t. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | You will not do’t for all the world, I [[Hope\|hope]].<br>You are [[Well\|well]] favoured, and your looks foreshow<br>You have a gentle heart. I saw you lately<br>When you caught hurt in parting two that fought.<br>Good sooth, it showed [[Well\|well]] in you. Do so now.<br>Your lady seeks my life. Come you between,<br>And save poor me, the weaker. |
| *(didascalia)* | LEONINE drawing out his [[Sword\|sword]] I am sworn, |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | And will dispatch. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Pirates running |
| FIRST PIRATE | Hold, [[Villain\|villain]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Leonine runs away and hides behind the tomb |
| SECOND PIRATE | A prize, a prize. |
| THIRD PIRATE | Half-part, mates, half-part. Come, let’s have her aboard suddenly. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Pirates carrying [[Marina]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Leonine steals back |
| LEONINE | These roguing thieves serve the great pirate Valdes.<br>An they have seized [[Marina]], let her go.<br>There’s no [[Hope\|hope]] she’ll return. I’ll swear she’s dead<br>And thrown into the sea; but I’ll see further.<br>Perhaps they will but please themselves upon her,<br>Not carry her aboard. If she remain,<br>Whom they have ravished must by me be slain. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit. The tomb is concealed |
| *(didascalia)* | Sc. 16 A brothel sign.] Enter the Pander, his wife the Bawd, and their man Boult |
| PANDER | Boult. |
| BOULT | Sir. |
| PANDER | Search the market narrowly. Mytilene is full of gallants. We lose too much money this mart by being wenchless. |
| BAWD | We were never so much out of creatures. We have but poor three, and they can do no more than they can do, and they with continual action are even as good as rotten. |
| PANDER | Therefore let’s have fresh ones, whate’er we pay for them. If there be not a [[Conscience\|conscience]] to be used in every trade, we shall never prosper. |
| BAWD | Thou sayst true. ’Tis not our bringing up of poor bastards—as I think I have brought up some eleven— |
| BOULT | Ay, to eleven, and brought them down again. But shall I search the market? |
| BAWD | What else, man? The stuff we have, a strong [[Wind\|wind]] will blow it to pieces, they are so pitifully sodden. |
| PANDER | Thou sayst true. They’re too unwholesome, o’ [[Conscience\|conscience]]. The poor Transylvanian is dead that lay with the little baggage. |
| BOULT | Ay, she quickly pooped him, she made him roast meat for worms. But I’ll go search the market. Exit |
| PANDER | Three or four thousand chequins were as pretty a proportion to live quietly, and so give over. |
| BAWD | Why to give over, I pray you? Is it a shame to get when we are old? |
| PANDER | O, our credit comes not in like the commodity, nor the commodity wages not with the danger. Therefore if in our youths we could pick up some pretty estate, ‘twere not amiss to keep our door hatched. Besides, the sore terms we stand upon with the gods will be strong with us for giving o’er. |
| BAWD | Come, other sorts offend as [[Well\|well]] as we. PANDER As [[Well\|well]] as we? Ay, and better too; we offend worse. Neither is our profession any mystery, it’s no calling. But here comes Boult. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Boult with the Pirates and [[Marina]] |
| *(didascalia)* | BOULT to the Pirates Come your ways, my masters, you say she’s a virgin? |
| BAWD | A PIRATE O sir, we [[Doubt\|doubt]] it not. |
| BOULT | (to Pander) Master, I have gone through for this piece you see. If you like her, so; if not, I have lost my earnest. |
| BAWD | Boult, has she any qualities? |
| BOULT | She has a good face, speaks [[Well\|well]], and has excellent good clothes. There’s no farther necessity of qualities can make her be refused. |
| BAWD | What’s her price, Boult? |
| BOULT | I cannot be bated one doit of a hundred sesterces. |
| PANDER | (to Pirates) [[Well]], follow me, my masters. You shall have your money presently. (To Bawd) Wife, take her in, instruct her what she has to do, that she may not be raw in her entertainment. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Pander and Pirates |
| BAWD | Boult, take you the marks of her, the colour of her hair, complexion, height, her age, with warrant of her virginity, and cry ‘He that will give most shall have her first.’ Such a maidenhead were no cheap thing if men were as they have been. Get this done as I command you. |
| BOULT | Performance shall follow. Exit |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Alack that Leonine was so slack, so slow.<br>He should have struck, not spoke; or that these pirates,<br>Not enough barbarous, had but o’erboard thrown me<br>To seek my mother. |
| BAWD | Why lament you, pretty one? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | That I am pretty. |
| BAWD | Come, the gods have done their part in you. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | I accuse them not. |
| BAWD | You are light into my hands, where you are like to live. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | The more my fault<br>To scape his hands where I was like to die. |
| BAWD | Ay, and you shall live in pleasure. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | No. |
| BAWD | Yes, indeed shall you, and taste gentlemen of all fashions. You shall fare [[Well\|well]]. You shall have the difference of all complexions. What, do you stop your ears? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Are you a woman? |
| BAWD | What would you have me be an I be not a woman? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | An honest woman, or not a woman. |
| BAWD | Marry, whip the gosling! I think I shall have something to do with you. Come, you’re a young foolish sapling, and must be bowed as I would have you. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | The gods defend me! |
| BAWD | If it please the gods to defend you by men, then men must comfort you, men must feed you, men must stir you up. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Boult |
| BAWD | Now, sir, hast thou cried her through the market? BOULT I have cried her almost to the number of her hairs.<br>I have drawn her picture with my voice. |
| BAWD | And I prithee tell me, how dost thou find the inclination of the people, especially of the younger sort? |
| BOULT | [[Faith]], they listened to me as they would have hearkened to their fathers’ testament. There was a Spaniard’s mouth watered as he went to bed to her very description. |
| BAWD | We shall have him here tomorrow with his best ruff on. |
| BOULT | Tonight, tonight. But mistress, do you know the French knight that cowers i’ the hams? |
| BAWD | Who, Monsieur Veroles? |
| BOULT | Ay, he. He offered to cut a caper at the proclamation, but he made a groan at it, and swore he would see her tomorrow. |
| BAWD | [[Well]], [[Well\|well]], as for him, he brought his disease hither. Here he does but repair it. I know he will come in our [[Shadow\|shadow]] to scatter his crowns of the sun. |
| BOULT | [[Well]], if we had of every nation a traveller, we should lodge them all with this sign. |
| BAWD | (to [[Marina]]) Pray you, come hither a while. You have fortunes coming upon you. Mark me, you must seem to do that fearfully which you commit willingly, to despise profit where you have most gain. To weep that you live as ye do makes pity in your [[lovers]]. Seldom but that pity begets you a good opinion, and that opinion a mere profit. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | I understand you not. |
| BOULT | (to Bawd) O, take her [[Home\|home]], mistress, take her [[Home\|home]]. These blushes of hers must be quenched with some present practice. |
| BAWD | Thou sayst true, i’[[Faith\|faith]], so they must, for your bride goes to that with shame which is her way to go with warrant. |
| BOULT | [[Faith]], some do and some do not. But mistress, if I have bargained for the joint— |
| BAWD | Thou mayst cut a morsel off the spit. |
| BOULT | I may so. |
| BAWD | Who should deny it? (To [[Marina]]) Come, young one, I like the manner of your garments [[Well\|well]]. |
| BOULT | Ay, by my [[Faith\|faith]], they shall not be changed yet. |
| BAWD | (giving him money) Boult, spend thou that in the town. Report what a sojourner we have. You’ll lose nothing by custom. When [[Nature\|nature]] framed this piece she meant thee a good turn. Therefore say what a paragon she is, and thou reapest the harvest out of thine own setting forth. |
| BOULT | I warrant you, mistress, thunder shall not so awake the beds of eels as my giving out her [[Beauty\|beauty]] stirs up the lewdly inclined. I’ll bring [[Home\|home]] some tonight. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| BAWD | Come your ways, follow me. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | If fires be hot, knives sharp, or waters deep,<br>Untied I still my virgin knot will keep.<br>[[Diana]] aid my purpose. |
| BAWD | What have we to do with [[Diana]]? Pray you, will you go with me? |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt. The sign is removed |
| *(didascalia)* | Sc. 17 Enter in mourning garments [[Cleon]] and [[Dionyza]] [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] |
| BAWD | Why, are you foolish? Can it be undone? |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | O [[Dionyza]], such a piece of slaughter<br>The sun and [[Moon\|moon]] ne’er looked upon. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | I think you’ll turn a [[Child\|child]] again. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | Were I chief lord of all this spacious world<br>I’d give it to undo the deed. A lady<br>Much less in [[Blood\|blood]] than virtue, yet a princess<br>To equal any single [[Crown\|crown]] o‘th’ earth<br>I’th’ justice of compare. O [[Villain\|villain]] Leonine,<br>Whom thou hast poisoned too,<br>If thou hadst drunk to him ‘t’ad been a kindness<br>Becoming [[Well\|well]] thy fact. What canst thou say<br>When noble [[Pericles]] demands his [[Child\|child]]? |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | That she is dead. Nurses are not the fates.<br>To foster is not ever to preserve.<br>She died at [[Night\|night]]. I’ll say so. Who can [[Cross\|cross]] it,<br>Unless you play the pious [[Innocent\|innocent]]<br>And, for an honest attribute, cry out<br>‘She died by foul play.’ |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | O, go to. [[Well]], [[Well\|well]],<br>Of all the faults beneath the heav’ns the gods<br>Do like this worst. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | Be one of those that thinks<br>The petty wrens of Tarsus will fly hence<br>And open this to [[Pericles]]. I do shame<br>To think of what a noble strain you are,<br>And of how cowed a spirit. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | To such proceeding<br>Whoever but his approbation added,<br>Though not his prime consent, he did not flow<br>From honourable sources. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | Be it so, then.<br>Yet none does know but you how she came dead,<br>Nor none can know, Leonine being gone.<br>She did distain my [[Child\|child]], and stood between<br>Her and her fortunes. None would look on her,<br>But cast their gazes on [[Marina]]’s face<br>Whilst ours was blurted at, and held a malkin<br>Not worth the [[Time\|time]] of day. It pierced me through,<br>And though you call my course unnatural,<br>You not your [[Child\|child]] [[Well\|well]] loving, yet I find<br>It greets me as an enterprise of kindness<br>Performed to your sole daughter. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | Heavens forgive it. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | And as for [[Pericles]],<br>What should he say? We wept after her hearse,<br>And yet we mourn. Her monument<br>Is almost finished, and her epitaphs<br>In glitt‘ring golden characters express<br>A gen’ral praise to her and care in us,<br>At whose expense ’tis done. |
| [[Cleon\|CLEON]] | Thou art like the harpy, Which, to betray, dost, with thine angel face, Seize in thine eagle talons. |
| [[Dionyza\|DIONYZA]] | Ye’re like one that superstitiously<br>Do swear to th’ gods that [[Winter\|winter]] kills the flies,<br>But yet I know you’ll do as I advise. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 18 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Gower]] |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | Thus [[Time\|time]] we waste, and long leagues make we short,<br>Sail seas in cockles, have and wish but for‘t,<br>Making to take [[Imagination\|imagination]]<br>From bourn to bourn, region to region.<br>By you being pardoned, we commit no crime<br>To use one language in each sev’ral clime<br>Where our scene seems to live. I do beseech you<br>To learn of me, who stand i’th’ gaps to teach you<br>The stages of our story: [[Pericles]]<br>Is now again thwarting the wayward seas,<br>Attended on by many a lord and knight,<br>To see his daughter, all his life’s delight.<br>Old Helicanus goes along. Behind<br>Is left to govern, if you bear in mind,<br>Old Aeschines, whom Helicanus late<br>Advanced in Tyre to great and high estate.<br>[[Well]] sailing ships and bounteous winds have brought<br>This [[King\|king]] to Tarsus—think his pilot thought;<br>So with his steerage shall your thoughts go on—<br>To fetch his daughter [[Home\|home]], who first is gone.<br>Like motes and shadows see them move a while;<br>Your ears unto your eyes I’ll reconcile.<br>Dumb show. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Pericles]] at one door with all his train, [[Cleon]] |
| *(didascalia)* | and [[Dionyza]] in mourning garments at the other. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Cleon]] draws the curtain and shows [[Pericles]] the |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | tomb, whereat [[Pericles]] makes lamentation, puts on<br><br>sack-cloth, and in a mighty passion departs,<br><br>followed by his train. [[Cleon]] and [[Dionyza]] depart at<br><br>the other door<br>See how belief may suffer by foul show.<br>This borrowed passion stands for true-owed woe,<br>And [[Pericles]], in sorrow all devoured,<br>With sighs shot through, and biggest [[Tears\|tears]] o‘ershow’red,<br>[[Leaves]] Tarsus, and again embarks. He swears<br>Never to wash his face nor cut his hairs.<br>He puts on sack-cloth, and to sea. He bears<br>A tempest which his mortal vessel [[Tears\|tears]],<br>And yet he rides it out. Now please you wit<br>The epitaph is for [[Marina]] writ<br>By wicked [[Dionyza]].<br>He reads [[Marina]]’s epitaph on the tomb<br>‘The fairest, sweetest, best lies here,<br>Who withered in her [[spring]] of year.<br>In [[Nature\|nature]]’s [[Garden\|garden]], though by growth a bud,<br>She was the chiefest flower: she was good.’<br>No visor does become black villainy<br>So [[Well\|well]] as soft and tender flattery.<br>Let [[Pericles]] believe his daughter’s dead<br>And bear his courses to be ordered<br>By Lady Fortune, while our scene must play<br>His daughter’s woe and heavy [[Well\|well]]-a-day<br>In her unholy service. Patience then,<br>And think you now are all in Mytilene. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Sc. 19 A brothel sign. Enter two Gentlemen |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Did you ever hear the like? |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | No, nor never shall do in such a place as this, she being once gone. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | But to have divinity preached there—did you ever dream of such a thing? |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | No, no. Come, I am for no more bawdy houses. Shall ’s go hear the vestals sing? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | I’ll do anything now that is virtuous, but I am out of the [[Road\|road]] of rutting for ever. Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Pander, Bawd, and Boult |
| PANDER | [[Well]], I had rather than twice the worth of her she had ne’er come here. |
| BAWD | Fie, fie upon her, she’s able to freeze the god Priapus and undo the whole of generation. We must either get her ravished or be rid of her. When she should do for clients her fitment and do me the kindness of our profession, she has me her quirks, her reasons, her master reasons, her prayers, her knees, that she would make a puritan of the devil if he should cheapen a kiss of her. |
| BOULT | [[Faith]], I must ravish her, or she’ll disfurnish us of all our cavalleria and make our swearers priests. |
| PANDER | Now, the pox upon her green-sickness for me. BAWD [[Faith]], there’s no way to be rid on’t but by the way to the pox. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lysimachus, disguised |
| PANDER | Here comes the Lord Lysimachus, disguised. |
| BOULT | We should have both lord and loon if the peevish baggage would but give way to custom. |
| LYSIMACHUS | How now, how a dozen of virginities? BAWD Now, the gods to-bless your [[Honour\|honour]]! |
| BOULT | I am glad to see your [[Honour\|honour]] in good health. |
| LYSIMACHUS | You may so. ’Tis the better for you that your resorters stand upon sound legs. How now, wholesome iniquity have you, that a man may deal withal and defy the surgeon? |
| BAWD | We have here one, sir, if she woutd—but there never came her like in Mytilene. |
| LYSIMACHUS | If she’d do the deed of darkness, thou wouldst say. |
| *(didascalia)* | BAWD Your [[Honour\|honour]] knows what ’tis to say [[Well\|well]] enough. LYSIMACHUS [[Well]], call forth, call forth. Exit Pander BOULT For flesh and [[Blood\|blood]], sir, [[White and Red\|white and red]], you shall see a [[Rose\|rose]]. And she were a [[Rose\|rose]] indeed, if she had but— |
| LYSIMACHUS | What, prithee? |
| BOULT | O sir, I can be modest. |
| LYSIMACHUS | That dignifies the renown of a bawd no less than it gives a good report to a noble to be chaste. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Pander with [[Marina]] |
| BAWD | Here comes that which grows to the stalk, never plucked yet, I can assure you. Is she not a fair creature? |
| LYSIMACHUS | [[Faith]], she would serve after a long voyage at sea. [[Well]], there’s for you. Leave us. |
| *(didascalia)* | He pays the Bawd |
| BAWD | I beseech your [[Honour\|honour]] give me leave: a word, and I’ll have done presently. |
| LYSIMACHUS | I beseech you, do. |
| BAWD | (aside to [[Marina]]) First, I would have you note this is an honourable man. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | I [[Desire\|desire]] to find him so, that I may honourably know him. |
| BAWD | Next, he’s the governor of this country, and a man whom I am bound to. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | If he govern the country you are bound to him indeed, but how honourable he is in that, I know not. |
| BAWD | Pray you, without any more virginal fencing, will you use him kindly? He will line your apron with gold. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | What he will do graciously I will thankfully receive. LYSIMACHUS (to Bawd) Ha’ you done? |
| BAWD | My lord, she’s not paced yet. You must take some pains to work her to your manège. (To Boult and Pander) Come, we will leave his [[Honour\|honour]] and hers together. Go thy ways. Exeunt Pander, Bawd, and Boult |
| LYSIMACHUS | Fair one, how long have you been at this trade? [[Marina\|MARINA]] What trade, sir? |
| LYSIMACHUS | I cannot name it but I shall offend. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | I cannot be offended with my trade.<br>Please you to name it. |
| LYSIMACHUS | How long have you been<br>Of this profession? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | E’er since I can remember. |
| LYSIMACHUS | Did you go to’t so young? Were you a gamester<br>At five, or seven? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Earlier too, sir,<br>If now I be one. |
| LYSIMACHUS | Why, the [[house]] you dwell in<br>Proclaimeth you a creature of sale. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | And do you know this [[house]] to be a place<br>Of such resort and will come into it?<br>I hear say you’re of honourable [[Blood\|blood]],<br>And are the governor of this whole province. |
| LYSIMACHUS | What, hath your principal informed you who I am? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Who is my principal? |
| LYSIMACHUS | Why, your herb-woman;<br>She that sets seeds of shame, roots of iniquity. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Marina]] weeps |
| LYSIMACHUS | O, you’ve heard something of my pow‘r, and so<br>Stand off aloof for a more serious wooing.<br>But I protest to thee,<br>Pretty one, my authority can wink<br>At blemishes, or can on faults look friendly;<br>Or my displeasure punish at my pleasure,<br>From which displeasure, not thy [[Beauty\|beauty]] shall<br>Privilege thee, nor my affection, which<br>Hath drawn me here, abate with further ling’ring.<br>Come bring me to some private place. Come, come. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Let not authority, which teaches you<br>To govern others, be the means to make you<br>Misgovern much yourself.<br>If you were born to [[Honour\|honour]], show it now;<br>If put upon you, make the judgement good<br>That thought you worthy of it. What reason’s in<br>Your justice, who hath power over all,<br>To undo any? If you take from me<br>Mine [[Honour\|honour]], you’re like him that makes a gap<br>Into forbidden ground, whom after<br>Too many enter, and of all their evils<br>Yourself are guilty. My life is yet unspotted;<br>My chastity unstained ev’n in thought.<br>Then if your violence deface this building,<br>The workmanship of heav‘n, you do kill your [[Honour\|honour]],<br>Abuse your justice, and impoverish me.<br>My yet good lord, if there be [[Fire\|fire]] before me,<br>Must I straight fly and burn myself? Suppose this<br>[[house]]—<br>Which too too many feel such houses are—<br>Should be the doctor’s patrimony, and<br>The surgeon’s feeding; follows it, that I<br>Must needs infect myself to give them maint’nance? |
| LYSIMACHUS | How’s this, how’s this? Some more. Be sage. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Marina\|MARINA]] kneeling For me |
| LYSIMACHUS | That am a maid, though most ungentle fortune<br>Have franked me in this sty, where since I came<br>Diseases have been sold dearer than physic—<br>That the gods would set me free from this unhallowed<br>place,<br>Though they did change me to the meanest bird<br>That flies i’th’ purer air! |
| *(didascalia)* | LYSIMACHUS moved I did not think |
| LYSIMACHUS | Thou couldst have spoke so [[Well\|well]], ne’er dreamt thou<br>couldst. |
| *(didascalia)* | He lifts her up with his hands |
| LYSIMACHUS | Though I brought hither a corrupted mind,<br>Thy speech hath altered it, |
| *(didascalia)* | He wipes the wet from her eyes and my foul thoughts |
| LYSIMACHUS | Thy [[Tears\|tears]] so [[Well\|well]] hath laved that they’re now white.<br>I came here meaning but to pay the price,<br>A piece of gold for thy virginity;<br>Here’s twenty to relieve thine honesty.<br>Persever still in that clear way thou goest,<br>And the gods strengthen thee. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | The good gods preserve you! |
| LYSIMACHUS | The very doors and windows savour vilely.<br>Fare thee [[Well\|well]]. Thou art a piece of virtue,<br>The best wrought up that ever [[Nature\|nature]] made,<br>And I [[Doubt\|doubt]] not thy training hath been noble.<br>A curse upon him, die he like a thief,<br>That robs thee of thy [[Honour\|honour]]. Hold, here’s more gold.<br>If thou dost hear from me, it shall be for thy good. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Boult standing ready at the door, making his obeisance unto him as Lysimachus should go out |
| BOULT | I beseech your [[Honour\|honour]], one piece for me. |
| LYSIMACHUS | Avaunt, thou damned door-keeper!<br>Your [[house]], but for this virgin that doth prop it,<br>Would sink and overwhelm you. Away. Exit |
| BOULT | How’s this? We must take another course with you. If your peevish chastity, which is not worth a breakfast in the cheapest country under the cope, shall undo a whole household, let me be gelded like a spaniel. Come your ways. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Whither would you have me? |
| BOULT | I must have your maidenhead taken off, or the common executioner shall do it. We’ll have no more gentlemen driven away. Come your ways, I say. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Bawd and Pander |
| BAWD | How now, what’s the matter? |
| BOULT | Worse and worse, mistress, she has here spoken holy words to the Lord Lysimachus. |
| BAWD | O, abominable! |
| BOULT | She makes our profession as it were to stink afore the face of the gods. |
| BAWD | Marry hang her up for ever! |
| BOULT | The nobleman would have dealt with her like a nobleman, and she sent him away as cold as a snowball, saying his prayers, too. |
| *(didascalia)* | PANDER Boult, take her away. Use her at thy pleasure. Crack the ice of her virginity, and make the rest malleable. |
| BOULT | An if she were a thornier piece of ground than she is, she shall be ploughed. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Hark, hark, you gods! |
| BAWD | She conjures. Away with her! Would she had never come within my doors.—Marry, hang you!—She’s born to undo us.—Will you not go the way of womenkind? Marry, come up, my dish of chastity with rosemary and bays. Exeunt Bawd and Pander |
| *(didascalia)* | BOULT catching her rashly by the hand Come, mistress, come your way with me. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Whither wilt thou have me? |
| BOULT | To take from you the [[Jewel\|jewel]] you hold so dear. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Prithee, tell me one thing first. |
| BOULT | Come, now, your one thing. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | What canst thou wish thine enemy to be? |
| BOULT | Why, I could wish him to be my master, or rather my mistress. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Neither of these can be so bad as thou art,<br>Since they do better thee in their command.<br>Thou hold‘st a place the painèd’st fiend of hell<br>Would not in reputation change with thee,<br>Thou damned doorkeeper to ev‘ry coistrel<br>That comes enquiring for his Tib.<br>To th’ choleric fisting of ev’ry rogue<br>Thy ear is liable. Thy food is such<br>As hath been belched on by infected lungs. |
| BOULT | What would you have me do? Go to the wars, would you, where a man may serve seven years for the loss of a leg, and have not money enough in the end to buy him a wooden one? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Do anything but this thou dost. Empty<br>Old receptacles or common sew’rs of filth,<br>Serve by indenture to the public hangman—<br>Any of these are yet better than this.<br>For what thou professest a baboon, could he speak,<br>Would own a name too dear. Here’s gold for thee.<br>If that thy master would make gain by me,<br>Proclaim that I can sing, weave, sew, and [[Dance\|dance]],<br>With other virtues which I’ll keep from boast,<br>And I will undertake all these to teach.<br>I [[Doubt\|doubt]] not but this populous [[City\|city]] will<br>Yield many scholars. |
| BOULT | But can you teach all this you speak of? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Prove that I cannot, take me [[Home\|home]] again<br>And prostitute me to the basest groom<br>That doth frequent your [[house]]. |
| BOULT | [[Well]], I will see what I can do for thee. If I can place thee, I will. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | But amongst honest women. |
| BOULT | [[Faith]], my acquaintance lies little amongst them; but since my master and mistress hath bought you, there’s no going but by their consent. Therefore I will make them acquainted with your purpose, and I [[Doubt\|doubt]] not but I shall find them tractable enough. Come, I’ll do for thee what I can. Come your ways. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt. The sign is removed |
| BOULT | Sc. 20 Enter [[Gower]] |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | [[Marina]] thus the brothel scapes, and chances<br>Into an honest [[house]], our story says.<br>She sings like one immortal, and she dances<br>As goddess-like to her admired lays.<br>Deep clerks she dumbs, and with her nee‘le composes<br>[[Nature]]’s own shape, of bud, bird, branch, or berry,<br>That e’en her art sisters the natural roses.<br>Her inkle, silk, twin with the rubied cherry;<br>That pupils lacks she none of noble race,<br>Who pour their bounty on her, and her gain<br>She gives the cursed Bawd. Here we her place,<br>And to her father turn our thoughts again.<br>We left him on the sea. Waves there him tossed,<br>Whence, driven tofore the winds, he is arrived<br>Here where his daughter dwells, and on this coast<br>Suppose him now at anchor. The [[City\|city]] strived<br>God Neptune’s annual feast to keep, from whence<br>Lysimachus our Tyrian [[Ship\|ship]] espies,<br>His banners sable, trimmed with rich expense;<br>And to him in his barge with fervour hies.<br>In your supposing once more put your sight;<br>Of heavy [[Pericles]] think this the barque,<br>Where what is done in action, more if might,<br>Shall be discovered. Please you sit and hark. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Sc. 21 Enter Helicanus above; below, enter to him at the first door two Sailors, one of Tyre, the other of Mytilene |
| SAILOR OF TYRE | (to Sailor of Mytilene)<br>Lord Helicanus can resolve you, sir.<br>(To Helicanus) There is a barge put off from Mytilene.<br>In it, Lysimachus, the governor,<br>Who craves to come aboard. What is your will? |
| HELICANUS | That he have his. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Sailor of Mytilene at first door Call up some gentlemen. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Helicanus above |
| *(didascalia)* | SAILOR OF TYRE |
| HELICANUS | Ho, my lord calls! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter from below the stage two or three Gentlemen; to them, enter Helicanus |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | What is your lordship’s pleasure? |
| HELICANUS | Gentlemen, some of worth would come aboard.<br>I pray you, greet him fairly. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lysimachus at first door, with the Sailor and Lords of Mytilene |
| *(didascalia)* | SAILOR OF MYTILENE (to Lysimachus) |
| HELICANUS | This is the man that can in aught resolve you. |
| LYSIMACHUS | (to Helicanus)<br>Hail, reverend sir; the gods preserve you! |
| HELICANUS | And you, sir, to outlive the age I am,<br>And die as I would do. |
| LYSIMACHUS | You wish me [[Well\|well]].<br>I am the governor of Mytilene;<br>Being on shore, honouring of Neptune’s triumphs,<br>Seeing this goodly vessel ride before us,<br>I made to it to know of whence you are. |
| HELICANUS | Our vessel is of Tyre, in it our [[King\|king]],<br>A man who for this three months hath not spoken<br>To anyone, nor taken sustenance<br>But to prorogue his grief. |
| LYSIMACHUS | Upon what ground grew his distemp’rature? |
| HELICANUS | ’Twould be too tedious to tell it over,<br>But the main grief springs from the precious loss<br>Of a belovèd daughter and a wife. |
| LYSIMACHUS | May we not see him? |
| HELICANUS | See him, sir, you may,<br>But bootless is your sight. He will not speak<br>To any. |
| LYSIMACHUS | Let me yet obtain my wish. |
| HELICANUS | Behold him.<br>Helicanus draws a curtain, revealing [[Pericles]] lying upon a couch with a long overgrown beard, diffused hair, undecent nails on his fingers, and attired in sack-cloth<br>This was a goodly person<br>Till the disaster of one mortal [[Night\|night]]<br>Drove him to this. |
| LYSIMACHUS | (to [[Pericles]])<br>Sir, [[King]], all hail. Hail, royal sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Pericles]] shrinks himself down upon his pillow |
| HELICANUS | It is in vain. He will not speak to you. |
| LORD OF MYTILENE | Sir, we have a maid in Mytilene I durst wager<br>Would win some words of him. |
| LYSIMACHUS | ’Tis [[Well\|well]] bethought.<br>She questionless, with her sweet harmony<br>And other choice attractions, would alarum<br>And make a batt‘ry through his deafened ports,<br>Which now are midway stopped. She in all happy,<br>As the fair’st of all, among her fellow maids<br>Dwells now i’th’ leafy shelter that abuts<br>Against the island’s side. Go fetch her hither. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Lord |
| HELICANUS | Sure, all effectless; yet nothing we’ll omit<br>That bears recov’ry’s name. But since your kindness<br>We have stretched thus far, let us beseech you<br>That for our gold we may provision have,<br>Wherein we are not destitute for want,<br>But weary for the staleness. |
| LYSIMACHUS | O sir, a courtesy<br>Which if we should deny, the most just gods<br>For every graft would send a caterpillar,<br>And so inflict our province. Yet once more<br>Let me entreat to know at large the cause<br>Of your [[King\|king]]’s sorrow. |
| HELICANUS | Sit, sir. I will recount it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lord with [[Marina]] and another maid |
| HELICANUS | But see, I am prevented. |
| LYSIMACHUS | O, here’s the lady that I sent for.—<br>Welcome, fair one.—Is’t not a goodly presence? HELICANUS She’s a gallant lady. |
| LYSIMACHUS | She’s such a one that, were I [[Well\|well]] assured<br>Came of gentle kind or noble stock, I’d wish<br>No better choice to think me rarely wed.—<br>Fair one, all goodness that consists in bounty<br>Expect e’en here, where is a kingly patient;<br>If that thy prosperous and artificial feat<br>Can draw him but to answer thee in aught,<br>Thy sacred physic shall receive such pay<br>As thy desires can wish. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Sir, I will use<br>My utmost skill in his recure, provided<br>That none but I and my companion maid<br>Be suffered to come near him. |
| LYSIMACHUS | (to the others) Let us leave her, |
| *(didascalia)* | And the gods prosper her. The men stand aside |
| LYSIMACHUS | The [[Song]] |
| *(didascalia)* | LYSIMACHUS coming forward Marked he your music? MAID |
| LYSIMACHUS | No, nor looked on us. |
| LYSIMACHUS | (to the others) See, she will speak to him. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | (to [[Pericles]])<br>Hail, sir; my lord, lend ear. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Hmh, ha! |
| *(didascalia)* | He roughly repulses her |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | I am a maid,<br>My lord, that ne‘er before invited eyes,<br>But have been gazed on like a comet. She speaks,<br>My lord, that maybe hath endured a grief<br>Might equal yours, if both were justly weighed.<br>Though wayward fortune did malign my state,<br>My derivation was from ancestors<br>Who stood equivalent with mighty kings,<br>But [[Time\|time]] hath rooted out my parentage,<br>And to the world and awkward casualties<br>Bound me in servitude. (Aside) I will desist.<br>But there is something glows upon my cheek,<br>And whispers in mine ear ‘Stay till he speak.’ |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | My fortunes, parentage, good parentage,<br>To equal mine? Was it not thus? What say you? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | I said if you did know my parentage,<br>My lord, you would not do me violence. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I do think so. Pray you, turn your eyes upon me.<br>You’re like something that—what countrywoman?<br>Here of these shores? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | No, nor of any shores,<br>Yet I was mortally brought forth, and am<br>No other than I seem. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] aside |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | I am great with woe, and shall deliver weeping.<br>My dearest wife was like this maid, and such<br>My daughter might have been. My [[Queen\|queen]]’s square<br>brows,<br>Her stature to an inch, as wand-like straight,<br>As silver-voiced, her eyes as [[Jewel\|jewel]]-like,<br>And cased as richly, in pace another Juno,<br>Who starves the ears she feeds, and makes them<br>hungry<br>The more she gives them speech.—Where do you live? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Where I am but a stranger. From the deck<br>You may discern the place. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Where were you bred, And how achieved you these endowments which You make more rich to owe? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | If I should tell My [[history]], it would seem like lies Disdained in the reporting. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Prithee speak.<br>Falseness cannot come from thee, for thou look‘st<br>Modest as justice, and thou seem’st a palace<br>For the crowned [[Truth\|truth]] to dwell in. I will believe thee,<br>And make my senses credit thy relation<br>To points that seem impossible. Thou show‘st<br>Like one I loved indeed. What were thy friends?<br>Didst thou not say, when I did push thee back—<br>Which was when I perceived thee—that thou cam’st<br>From good descending? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | So indeed I did. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Report thy parentage. I think thou said‘st<br>Thou hadst been tossed from wrong to injury,<br>And that thou thought’st thy griefs might equal mine,<br>If both were opened. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Some such thing I said,<br>And said no more but what my circumstance<br>Did warrant me was likely. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Tell thy story.<br>If thine considered prove the thousandth part<br>Of my [[Endurance\|endurance]], thou art a man, and I<br>Have suffered like a girl. Yet thou dost look<br>Like patience gazing on kings’ graves, and smiling<br>Extremity out of act. What were thy friends?<br>How lost thou them? Thy name, my most kind virgin?<br>Recount, I do beseech thee. Come, sit by me.<br>She sits |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | My name, sir, is [[Marina]]. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | O, I am mocked,<br>And thou by some incensed god sent hither<br>To make the world to laugh at me. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Patience, good sir,<br>Or here I’ll cease. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Nay, I’ll be patient.<br>Thou little know’st how thou dost startle me<br>To call thyself [[Marina]]. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | The name<br>Was given me by one that had some power:<br>My father, and a [[King\|king]]. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | How, a [[King\|king]]’s daughter, And called [[Marina]]? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | You said you would believe me, But not to be a troubler of your peace I will end here. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | But are you flesh and [[Blood\|blood]]?<br>Have you a working pulse and are no fairy?<br>Motion as [[Well\|well]]? Speak on. Where were you born,<br>And wherefore called [[Marina]]? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | Called [[Marina]]<br>For I was born at sea. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | At sea? What mother? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | My mother was the daughter of a [[King\|king]],<br>Who died when I was born, as my good [[Nurse\|nurse]]<br>Lychorida hath oft recounted weeping. |
| *(didascalia)* | O, stop there a little! Aside This is the rarest dream |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | That e’er dulled [[Sleep\|sleep]] did mock sad fools withal.<br>This cannot be my daughter, buried. [[Well]].<br>(To [[Marina]]) Where were you bred? I’ll hear you more<br>to th’ [[Bottom\|bottom]]<br>Of your story, and never interrupt you. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | You will scarce believe me. ‘Twere best I did give o’er. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I will believe you by the syllable<br>Of what you shall deliver. Yet give me leave.<br>How came you in these parts? Where were you bred? |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | The [[King]] my father did in Tarsus leave me,<br>Till cruel [[Cleon]], with his wicked wife,<br>Did seek to murder me, and wooed a [[Villain\|villain]]<br>To attempt the deed; who having drawn to do’t,<br>A crew of pirates came and rescued me.<br>To Mytilene they brought me. But, good sir,<br>What will you of me? Why do you weep? It may be<br>You think me an impostor. No, good [[Faith\|faith]],<br>I am the daughter to [[King]] [[Pericles]],<br>If good [[King]] [[Pericles]] be. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | rising Ho, Helicanus! |
| HELICANUS | (coming forward) Calls my lord? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Thou art a [[Grave\|grave]] and noble counsellor,<br>Most wise in gen’ral. Tell me if thou canst<br>What this maid is, or what is like to be,<br>That thus hath made me weep. |
| HELICANUS | I know not.<br>But here’s the regent, sir, of Mytilene<br>Speaks nobly of her. |
| LYSIMACHUS | She would never tell<br>Her parentage. Being demanded that,<br>She would sit still and weep. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | O Helicanus, strike me, honoured sir,<br>Give me a gash, put me to present [[Pain\|pain]],<br>Lest this great sea of joys rushing upon me<br>O‘erbear the shores of my [[Mortality\|mortality]]<br>And drown me with their sweetness! (To [[Marina]]) O,<br>come hither, |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Marina]] stands |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Thou that begett’st him that did thee beget,<br>Thou that wast born at sea, buried at Tarsus,<br>And found at sea again!—O Helicanus,<br>Down on thy knees, thank the holy gods as loud<br>As thunder threatens us, this is [[Marina]]!<br>(To [[Marina]]) What was thy mother’s name? Tell me<br>but that,<br>For [[Truth\|truth]] can never be confirmed enough,<br>Though doubts did ever [[Sleep\|sleep]]. |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | First, sir, I pray, What is your title? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I am [[Pericles]]<br>Of Tyre. But tell me now my drowned [[Queen\|queen]]’s name.<br>As in the rest thou hast been godlike perfect,<br>So prove but true in that, thou art my daughter,<br>The heir of kingdoms, and another life<br>To [[Pericles]] thy father. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Marina\|MARINA]] kneeling Is it no more |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | To be your daughter than to say my mother’s name?<br>[[Thaisa]] was my mother, who did end<br>The minute I began. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Now blessing on thee! Rise. Thou art my [[Child\|child]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Marina]] stands. He kisses her |
| *(didascalia)* | To attendants Give me fresh garments.—Mine own, |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Helicanus!<br>Not dead at Tarsus, as she should have been<br>By savage [[Cleon]]. She shall tell thee all,<br>When thou shalt kneel and justify in knowledge<br>She is thy very princess. Who is this? |
| HELICANUS | Sir, ’tis the governor of Mytilene,<br>Who, hearing of your [[Melancholy\|melancholy]] state,<br>Did come to see you. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | (to Lysimachus) I embrace you, sir.—<br>Give me my robes. |
| *(didascalia)* | He is attired in fresh robes |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I am wild in my beholding.<br>O heavens, bless my girl! |
| *(didascalia)* | Celestial music |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | But hark, what music?<br>Tell Helicanus, my [[Marina]], tell him<br>O’er point by point, for yet he seems to [[Doubt\|doubt]],<br>How sure you are my daughter. But what music? |
| HELICANUS | My lord, I hear none. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | None? The music of the spheres! List, my [[Marina]]. |
| LYSIMACHUS | (aside to the others)<br>It is not good to [[Cross\|cross]] him. Give him way. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Rar’st sounds. Do ye not hear? |
| LYSIMACHUS | Music, my lord? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I hear most heav’nly music.<br>It raps me unto list’ning, and thick slumber<br>Hangs upon mine eyelids. Let me rest.<br>He sleeps |
| LYSIMACHUS | A pillow for his head. |
| *(didascalia)* | To [[Marina]] and others Companion friends, |
| LYSIMACHUS | If this but answer to my just belief<br>I’ll [[Well\|well]] remember you. So leave him all. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Pericles]] |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Diana]] descends from the heavens |
| [[Diana\|DIANA]] | My temple stands in Ephesus. Hie thee thither,<br>And do upon mine altar sacrifice.<br>There when my maiden priests are met together,<br>At large discourse thy fortunes in this wise:<br>With a full voice before the people all,<br>Reveal how thou at sea didst lose thy wife.<br>To mourn thy crosses, with thy daughter‘s, call<br>And give them repetition to the life.<br>Perform my bidding, or thou liv’st in woe;<br>Do’t, and rest happy, by my silver bow.<br>Awake, and tell thy dream. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Diana]] ascends into the heavens |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Celestial Dian, goddess argentine,<br>I will obey thee. (Calling) Helicanus! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Helicanus, Lysimachus, and [[Marina]] |
| HELICANUS | Sir? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | My purpose was for Tarsus, there to strike<br>Th’inhospitable [[Cleon]], but I am<br>For other service first. Toward Ephesus<br>Turn our blown sails. Eftsoons I’ll tell thee why. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Helicanus |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Shall we refresh us, sir, upon your shore,<br>And give you gold for such provision<br>As our intents will need? |
| LYSIMACHUS | With all my heart, sir,<br>And when you come ashore I have a suit. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | You shall prevail, were it to woo my daughter,<br>For it seems you have been noble towards her. |
| LYSIMACHUS | Sir, lend me your arm. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Come, my [[Marina]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Pericles]] with Lysimachus at one arm, [[Marina]] at the other |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Sc. 22 Enter [[Gower]] |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | Now our sands are almost run;<br>More a little, and then dumb.<br>This my last boon give me,<br>For such kindness must relieve me,<br>That you aptly will suppose<br>What pageantry, what feats, what shows,<br>What minstrelsy and pretty din<br>The regent made in Mytilene<br>To greet the [[King]]. So [[Well\|well]] he thrived<br>That he is promised to be wived<br>To fair [[Marina]], but in no wise<br>Till he had done his sacrifice<br>As Dian bade, whereto being bound<br>The int’rim, pray you, all confound.<br>In feathered briefness sails are filled,<br>And wishes fall out as they’re willed.<br>At Ephesus the temple see: |
| *(didascalia)* | An altar, [[Thaisa]] and other vestals are revealed |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | Our [[King\|king]], and all his company. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Pericles]], [[Marina]], Lysimachus, Helicanus, Cerimon, with attendants |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | That he can hither come so soon<br>Is by your fancies’ thankful doom. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Gower]] stands aside |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Hail, Dian. To perform thy just command<br>I here confess myself the [[King]] of Tyre,<br>Who, frighted from my country, did espouse<br>The fair [[Thaisa]] |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Thaisa]] starts |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | at Pentapolis.<br>At sea in childbed died she, but brought forth<br>A maid [[Child\|child]] called [[Marina]], who, O goddess,<br>Wears yet thy silver liv‘ry. She at Tarsus<br>Was nursed with [[Cleon]], whom at fourteen years<br>He sought to murder, but her better [[Stars\|stars]]<br>Bore her to Mytilene, ’gainst whose shore riding<br>Her fortunes brought the maid aboard our barque,<br>Where, by her own most clear remembrance, she<br>Made known herself my daughter. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | Voice and favour—<br>You are, you are—O royal [[Pericles]]! She falls |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | What means the nun? She dies. Help, gentlemen! |
| CERIMON | Noble sir,<br>If you have told [[Diana]]’s altar true,<br>This is your wife. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Reverend appearer, no.<br>I threw her overboard with these same arms. |
| CERIMON | Upon this coast, I warr’nt you. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | ’Tis most certain. |
| CERIMON | Look to the lady. O, she’s but o’erjoyed.<br>Early one blustering morn this lady<br>Was thrown upon this shore. I oped the coffin,<br>Found there rich jewels, recovered her, and placed her<br>Here in [[Diana]]’s temple. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | May we see them? |
| CERIMON | Great sir, they shall be brought you to my [[house]],<br>Whither I invite you. Look, [[Thaisa]] is<br>Recovered. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | O, let me look upon him!<br>If he be none of mine, my sanctity<br>Will to my sense bend no licentious ear,<br>But curb it, spite of seeing. O, my lord,<br>Are you not [[Pericles]]? Like him you spake,<br>Like him you are. Did you not name a tempest,<br>A birth and [[Death\|death]]? |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | The voice of dead [[Thaisa]]! [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] That [[Thaisa]]<br>Am I, supposed dead and drowned. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] taking [[Thaisa]]’s hand Immortal Dian! |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | Now I know you better.<br>When we with [[Tears\|tears]] parted Pentapolis,<br>The [[King]] my father gave you such a ring. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | This, this! No more, you gods. Your present kindness<br>Makes my past miseries sports; you shall do [[Well\|well]]<br>That on the touching of her lips I may<br>Melt, and no more be seen.—O come, be buried<br>A second [[Time\|time]] within these arms. |
| *(didascalia)* | They embrace and kiss |
| [[Marina\|MARINA]] | (kneeling to [[Thaisa]]) My heart Leaps to be gone into my mother’s bosom. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Look who kneels here: flesh of thy flesh, [[Thaisa]],<br>Thy burden at the sea, and called [[Marina]]<br>For she was yielded there. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] embracing [[Marina]] Blessed, and mine own! HELICANUS kneeling to [[Thaisa]] |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Hail, madam, and my [[Queen\|queen]]. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | I know you not. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | You have heard me say, when I did fly from Tyre,<br>I left behind an ancient substitute.<br>Can you remember what I called the man?<br>I have named him oft. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | ’Twas Helicanus then. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Still confirmation.<br>Embrace him, dear [[Thaisa]]; this is he.<br>Now do I long to hear how you were found,<br>How possibly preserved, and who to thank—<br>Besides the gods—for this great miracle. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | Lord Cerimon, my lord. This is the man<br>Through whom the gods have shown their pow’r,<br>that can<br>From first to last resolve you. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | (to Cerimon) Reverend sir,<br>The gods can have no mortal officer<br>More like a god than you. Will you deliver<br>How this dead [[Queen\|queen]] re-lives? |
| CERIMON | I will, my lord.<br>Beseech you, first go with me to my [[house]],<br>Where shall be shown you all was found with her,<br>And told how in this temple she came placed,<br>No needful thing omitted. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Pure [[Diana]],<br>I bless thee for thy vision, and will offer<br>Nightly oblations to thee.—Beloved [[Thaisa]],<br>This prince, the fair betrothed of your daughter,<br>At Pentapolis shall marry her.<br>(To [[Marina]]) And now this ornament<br>Makes me look dismal will I clip to form,<br>And what this fourteen years no razor touched,<br>To grace thy [[Marriage\|marriage]] day I’ll beautify. |
| [[Thaisa\|THAISA]] | Lord Cerimon hath letters of good credit,<br>Sir, from Pentapolis: my father’s dead. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Heav’n make a star of him! Yet there, my [[Queen\|queen]],<br>We’ll celebrate their nuptials, and ourselves<br>Will in that kingdom spend our following days.<br>Our son and daughter shall in Tyrus reign.—<br>Lord Cerimon, we do our [[Longing\|longing]] stay<br>To hear the rest untold. Sir, lead ’s the way. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Gower]] |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | In [[Antiochus]] and his daughter you have heard<br>Of monstrous lust the due and just reward;<br>In [[Pericles]], his [[Queen\|queen]], and daughter seen,<br>Although assailed with fortune fierce and keen,<br>Virtue preserved from fell destruction’s blast,<br>Led on by heav’n, and crowned with joy at last.<br>In Helicanus may you [[Well\|well]] descry<br>A figure of [[Truth\|truth]], of [[Faith\|faith]], of loyalty.<br>In reverend Cerimon there [[Well\|well]] appears<br>The worth that learned [[Charity\|charity]] aye wears.<br>For wicked [[Cleon]] and his wife, when fame<br>Had spread their cursed deed to th’ honoured name<br>Of [[Pericles]], to rage the [[City\|city]] turn,<br>That him and his they in his palace burn.<br>The gods for murder seemed so content<br>To punish that, although not done, but meant.<br>So on your patience evermore attending,<br>New joy wait on you. Here our play has ending. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| ADDITIONAL PASSAGES | Q gives this more expansive version of [[Marina]]’s Epitaph (18.347):<br>’The fairest, sweetest, best lies here,<br>Who withered in her [[spring]] of year.<br>She was of Tyrus the [[King]]’s daughter,<br>On whom foul [[Death\|death]] hath made this slaughter.<br>[[Marina]] was she called, and at her birth<br>Thetis, being proud, swallowed some part o‘th’ earth;<br>Therefore the earth, fearing to be o’erflowed,<br>Hath Thetis’ birth-[[Child\|child]] on the heav‘ns bestowed,<br>Wherefore she does, and swears she’ll never stint,<br>Make raging batt’ry upon shores of flint.’ |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="scenes--scene_1" data-title="Pericles — Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/child,archetype/king,motif/city,motif/death,motif/desert,motif/desire,motif/dust,motif/fire,motif/flowers,motif/love,motif/marriage,motif/mirror,motif/nature,motif/night,motif/poison,motif/soul,motif/spring,motif/stars,motif/time,motif/well,motif/wind,concept/beauty,concept/body,concept/courage,concept/fear,concept/honour,concept/hope,concept/mercy,concept/mortality,concept/nature,concept/self,form/riddle,setting/desert,character/antiochus,character/gower,character/pericles,character/pistol"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Gower]] as Prologue |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | To sing a [[Song\|song]] that old was sung<br>From ashes ancient [[Gower]] is come,<br>Assuming man’s infirmities<br>To glad your ear and please your eyes.<br>It hath been sung at festivals,<br>On ember-eves and holy-ales,<br>And lords and ladies in their lives<br>Have read it for restoratives.<br>The purchase is to make men glorious,<br>Et bonum quo antiquius eo melius.<br><br>If you, born in these latter times<br>When wit’s more ripe, accept my rhymes,<br>And that to hear an old man sing<br>May to your wishes pleasure bring,<br>I life would wish, and that I might<br>Waste it for you like taper-light.<br>This’ Antioch, then; [[Antiochus]] the Great<br>Built up this [[City\|city]] for his chiefest seat,<br>The fairest in all Syria.<br>I tell you what mine authors say.<br>This [[King\|king]] unto him took a fere<br>Who died, and left a female heir<br>So buxom, blithe, and full of face<br>As heav’n had lent her all his grace,<br>With whom the father liking took,<br>And her to incest did provoke.<br>Bad [[Child\|child]], worse father, to entice his own<br>To evil should be done by none.<br>By custom what they did begin<br>Was with long use account’ no sin.<br>The [[Beauty\|beauty]] of this sinful dame<br>Made many princes thither frame<br>To seek her as a bedfellow,<br>In [[Marriage\|marriage]] pleasures playfellow,<br>Which to prevent he made a law<br>To keep her still, and men in awe,<br>That whoso asked her for his wife,<br>His [[Riddle\|riddle]] told not, lost his life.<br>So for her many a wight did die, |
| *(didascalia)* | A row of heads is revealed |
| [[Gower\|GOWER]] | As yon grim looks do testify.<br>What now ensues, to th’ judgement of your eye<br>I give, my cause who best can justify. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Sennet. Enter [[King]] [[Antiochus]], Prince [[Pericles]], and lords and peers in their richest ornaments |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Young Prince of Tyre, you have at large received<br>The danger of the task you undertake. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I have, [[Antiochus]], and with a [[Soul\|soul]]<br>Emboldened with the glory of her praise<br>Think [[Death\|death]] no hazard in this enterprise. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Music! |
| *(didascalia)* | Music sounds |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Bring in our daughter, clothèd like a bride<br>Fit for th’embracements ev’n of Jove himself,<br>At whose conception, till Lucina reigned,<br>[[Nature]] this dowry gave to glad her presence:<br>The senate-[[house]] of planets all did sit,<br>In her their best perfections to knit. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Antiochus]]’ Daughter |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | See where she comes, apparelled like the [[spring]],<br>Graces her subjects, and her thoughts the [[King\|king]]<br>Of ev’ry virtue gives renown to men;<br>Her face the book of praises, where is read<br>Nothing but curious pleasures, as from thence<br>Sorrow were ever razed and testy wrath<br>Could never be her mild companion.<br>You gods that made me man, and sway in [[Love\|love]],<br>That have inflamed [[Desire\|desire]] in my breast<br>To taste the fruit of yon celestial tree<br>Or die in the adventure, be my helps,<br>As I am son and servant to your will,<br>To compass such a boundless happiness. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Prince [[Pericles]]— |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | That would be son to great [[Antiochus]]. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Before thee stands this fair Hesperides,<br>With golden fruit, but dang’rous to be touched, |
| *(didascalia)* | He gestures towards the heads |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | For [[Death\|death]]-like dragons here affright thee hard. |
| *(didascalia)* | He gestures towards his daughter |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Her heav‘n-like face enticeth thee to view<br>Her countless glory, which [[Desert\|desert]] must gain;<br>And which without [[Desert\|desert]], because thine eye<br>Presumes to reach, all the whole heap must die.<br>Yon sometimes famous princes, like thyself<br>Drawn by report, advent’rous by [[Desire\|desire]],<br>Tell thee with speechless tongues and semblants<br>bloodless<br>That without covering save yon field of [[Stars\|stars]]<br>Here they stand, martyrs slain in Cupid’s wars,<br>And with dead cheeks advise thee to desist<br>From going on [[Death\|death]]’s net, whom none resist. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | [[Antiochus]], I thank thee, who hath taught<br>My frail [[Mortality\|mortality]] to know itself,<br>And by those fearful objects to prepare<br>This [[Body\|body]], like to them, to what I must;<br>For [[Death\|death]] remembered should be like a [[Mirror\|mirror]]<br>Who tells us life’s but breath, to trust it error.<br>I’ll make my will then, and, as sick men do,<br>Who know the world, see heav‘n, but feeling woe<br>Grip not at earthly joys as erst they did,<br>So I bequeath a happy peace to you<br>And all good men, as ev’ry prince should do;<br>My riches to the earth from whence they came,<br>(To the Daughter) But my unspotted [[Fire\|fire]] of [[Love\|love]] to you.<br>(To [[Antiochus]]) Thus ready for the way of life or [[Death\|death]],<br>I wait the sharpest blow, [[Antiochus]]. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Scorning advice, read the conclusion then, |
| *(didascalia)* | He angrily throws down the [[Riddle\|riddle]] |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Which read and not expounded, ’tis decreed,<br>As these before thee, thou thyself shalt bleed. |
| DAUGHTER | (to [[Pericles]])<br>Of all ‘sayed yet, mayst thou prove prosperous;<br>Of all ’sayed yet, I wish thee happiness. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Like a bold champion I assume the lists,<br>Nor ask advice of any other thought<br>But faithfulness and [[Courage\|courage]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | He takes up and reads aloud the [[Riddle\|riddle]] |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | I am no viper, yet I feed<br>On mother’s flesh which did me breed.<br>I sought a husband, in which labour<br>I found that kindness in a father.<br>He’s father, son, and husband mild;<br>I mother, wife, and yet his [[Child\|child]].<br>How this may be and yet in two,<br>As you will live resolve it you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sharp physic is the last. Aside But O, you powers |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | That gives heav’n countless eyes to view men’s acts,<br>Why cloud they not their sights perpetually<br>If this be true which makes me pale to read it? |
| *(didascalia)* | He gazes on the Daughter |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Fair glass of light, I loved you, and could still,<br>Were not this glorious casket stored with ill.<br>But I must tell you now my thoughts revolt,<br>For he’s no man on whom perfections wait<br>That, knowing sin within, will touch the gate.<br>You’re a fair viol, and your sense the strings<br>Who, fingered to make man his lawful music,<br>Would draw heav’n down and all the gods to hearken,<br>But, being played upon before your [[Time\|time]],<br>Hell only danceth at so harsh a chime.<br>Good sooth, I care not for you. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | Prince [[Pericles]], touch not, upon thy life,<br>For that’s an article within our law<br>As dang’rous as the rest. Your [[Time\|time]]’s expired.<br>Either expound now, or receive your sentence. |
| [[Pericles\|PERICLES]] | Great [[King]],<br>Few [[Love\|love]] to hear the sins they [[Love\|love]] to act.<br>‘Twould braid yourself too near for me to tell it.<br>Who has a book of all that monarchs do,<br>He’s more secure to keep it shut than shown,<br>For vice repeated, like the wand’ring [[Wind\|wind]],<br>Blows [[Dust\|dust]] in others’ eyes to spread itself;<br>And yet the end of all is bought thus dear,<br>The breath is gone, and the sore eyes see clear<br>To stop the air would hurt them. The blind mole casts<br>Copped hills towards heav’n to tell the earth is thronged<br>By man’s oppression, and the poor worm doth die for’t.<br>Kings are earth’s gods; in vice their law’s their will,<br>And if Jove stray, who dares say Jove doth ill?<br>It is enough you know, and it is fit,<br>What being more known grows worse, to smother it.<br>All [[Love\|love]] the womb that their first being bred;<br>Then give my tongue like leave to [[Love\|love]] my head. |
| [[Antiochus\|ANTIOCHUS]] | (aside)<br>Heav’n, that I had thy head! He’s found the meaning.<br>But I will gloze with him.—Young Prince of Tyre,<br>Though by the tenor of our strict edict,<br>Your exposition misinterpreting,<br>We might proceed to cancel of your days,<br>Yet [[Hope\|hope]], succeeding from so fair a tree<br>As your fair [[Self\|self]], doth tune us otherwise.<br>Forty days longer we do respite you,<br>If by which [[Time\|time]] our secret be undone,<br>This [[Mercy\|mercy]] shows we’ll joy in such a son.<br>And until then your entertain shall be<br>As doth befit your worth and our degree. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish.Exeunt all but [[Pericles]] |
